This is the **third occurrence** of the known `plan_commit` read-modify-write race (TASK-008 was the second); content is correct and block-confined, but the history again shows an ORCH commit carrying a builder's edit. Fix mechanically (re-diff before commit), not by instruction.

**All 7 acceptance criteria verified against independently quoted spec text** (TS §8.3 step 4 L296, §8.6 L326-L350 in full, FR-022 L122, FR-023 L123, FR-025 L125, §11 E5 L449-454), not GB's summary. (1) **Election is deterministic and genuinely a pure function** — `Arbiter.elect` is `min(peerId)` over the roster with no clock, no I/O and no insertion-order dependence (tested explicitly by `lexicographic, not insertion order`); given the same peer set every peer computes the same arbiter, which is what "deterministic" has to mean here. Re-election is recomputed on every `updateRoster`. (2) **The ≤ 500 ms self-heal bound is honoured by a genuinely elegant reading.** §8.6 L346 sets departure detection at 15 s (5 s × 3 misses), which cannot itself re-elect inside 500 ms; GB resolved this by making election instantaneous on roster change and letting the **TX_REQ retry budget** carry the bound — 150 ms × 3 = 450 ms ≤ 500 ms, so a requester whose arbiter vanished is answered by the new arbiter within the settle window. The test `arbiter loss self-heals: new arbiter grants within 500 ms` asserts exactly that, and pins `clock.now() - start <= FloorTiming.arbiterReelectionSettle`. Documented in README and dartdoc rather than left implicit. (3) Lease = TOT + 2 s, asserted at 45 s → 47000 ms and at the 30 s/120 s bounds. (4) Idempotent re-grant **keeps the remaining lease** (`defaultGrantLease - 1000` after 1 s), so a retry provably cannot extend TOT. (5) TOT warn at T−5 s and hard cut at 0 with the floor released, driven through the whole 30 s at minimum TOT. (6) Retry timing is exactly right: sends at t=0/150/300 ms and gives up at t=450 ms — 3 attempts, not 4. (7) Busy lockout defaults on.

**TASK-006's frozen constants are CONSUMED, not re-derived — the specific drift risk this task carried.** Verified by grep: `FloorTiming.defaultTot`, `.grantLease(_tot)`, `.txReqRetry`, `.txReqAttempts`, `.floorIdleDebounce` are all imported at their use sites, and `FloorPrio.normal/emergency` + `FloorDenyReason.busy/lockout` come from the frozen `messages.dart`. **There is not a single magic `62000`, `150`, `750` or `500` literal in `lib/core/floor/`.** GB used `FloorTiming.grantLease(_tot)` rather than the `defaultGrantLease` constant, so FR-023's configurable 30–120 s TOT yields a correct 32–122 s lease — it inherited TASK-006's own best decision rather than undoing it. The three constants GB *does* declare locally (`minTot` 30 s, `maxTot` 120 s, `totWarnLead` 5 s) are **necessary, not drift**: `FloorTiming` has no TOT range and no warn lead, because FR-023 defines them and §8.6's locked table does not. A `timing constants used by the engine match the locked §8.6 table` test pins all ten values plus the three local ones.

**Determinism is real, not claimed.** `floor_engine.dart` contains **zero** direct `DateTime.now()` and zero direct `Timer` — every time read and all five timers (retry, TOT-warn, TOT-cut, lease, idle) go through the injected `FloorClock`; the only `DateTime.now()`/`Timer` in the whole territory are the two lines inside `WallClock`. `test/core/floor/` contains **no** `Future.delayed`, `await Future`, `Timer` or `sleep` at all, and all 28 tests complete inside the reporter's `00:00` tick despite asserting 150 ms retries, 500 ms self-heal, 750 ms debounce and a full 30 s TOT. `VirtualClock` is a proper discrete-event simulator (deadline-ordered, `seq`-tiebroken for equal deadlines, with its own ordering test) rather than a counter. `LoopbackHub` re-entrancy is handled deliberately: nested sends are queued and drained at one depth so a grant that immediately produces TX_START cannot re-enter a sync broadcast controller mid-callback. No `skip:`/`solo:`/`@Skip`/`@Timeout` anywhere in `test/`.

**ORCH probe suite (9 adversarial scenarios written and run against the merged tree, then deleted) — the engine survived all of them.** (a) *Arbiter is itself the speaker when a remote emergency pre-empts* — the single most likely double-transmit hole, and untested by GB, since `a` (the arbiter) is a bystander in every one of its multi-peer tests. It **passes**: the pre-empted arbiter stops. Mechanism is indirect — the arbiter does not receive its own `TX_GRANT` (loopback correctly skips the sender), so teardown comes from the new speaker's `TX_START` via `_onRemoteStart`, which forces any local TX down. Sound, but see follow-up (1). (b) *EMG persistence* — pinned EMG survives a TOT hard cut, lease expiry and **10 minutes** of virtual clock, and clears **only** on explicit `EMG_CLR`; there is no timeout path anywhere (`_emg.clear` has exactly two call sites, both explicit). FR-025's "until the sender clears it" is implemented literally. (c) *Emergency-over-emergency* pre-empts correctly. (d) *Split roster / two self-appointed arbiters* — the one-voice invariant **held**, and for a good structural reason: `TX_GRANT` is broadcast, so the second arbiter learns the floor is taken and denies `BUSY`. (e) *Arbiter dies mid-transmission* — floor frees and the new arbiter grants. (f) Lease expiry genuinely **frees** the floor rather than merely flagging it: `_liveHolder` is time-checked on every read (lazy) **and** `_onLeaseExpired` clears state and emits the reducer event (eager), so a crashed speaker's floor is released on both paths.

**Consumes the TASK-004 reducer, does not duplicate it.** The engine holds no `RadioState` reference and never writes one — it emits `DispatchRadio(RadioEvent)` effects the host feeds to `RadioReducer`, proven end-to-end by a test that wires a real `RadioReducer` and asserts `idle → txRequest → tx → idle`. Effects are a single sealed stream (`GrantTone`, `DenyBuzz`, `TotWarn`, `TotCut`, `EmgPinned`, `EmgCleared`, `ArbiterChanged`, `FloorIdleSettled`), so SFX/haptics/UI subscribe without coupling — exactly the shape TASK-015/017 need.

**FOLLOW-UP (m) DID BITE, exactly as the TASK-006 review predicted — and GB's workaround is sound but explicitly temporary.** `RadioState` (frozen at `lib/core/state/**`) has no EMG flag, no station count and no active-speaker field, so the three pieces of state this engine owns and the face must render **cannot be expressed as reducer events**. GB routed them around the reducer instead of faking them into it: EMG lives in a floor-local `EmergencyPin` surfaced as `EmgPinned`/`EmgCleared` effects plus `isEmergencyPinned`/`emergencyPeer` getters; the active speaker is the `holder` getter; the arbiter is `arbiterId`/`isLocalArbiter` plus an `ArbiterChanged` effect. Probed directly: on an emergency pre-emption a bystander's reducer sees **only** `RemoteFloorStarted`, while `EmgPinned` and `ArbiterChanged` arrive as non-reducer effects. This is the **right** call for a frozen dependency — it is a one-way driver, so there is no read/write divergence *inside* the engine — but it means **TASK-017's face must merge two sources (RadioState + FloorEngine getters/effects) to render one visual state**, and the engine's private 4-state `_Phase` (idle/awaiting/tx/rx) is a second state machine that can drift from `RadioPhase` if the host ever dispatches floor-relevant events from elsewhere (e.g. TASK-015's PTT widget). **The (m) successor task owning `lib/core/state/**` must reconcile this**, and station count is not even exposed — `_peers` has no public getter, so `STN n` has no source at all today.

**Seven non-blocking follow-ups; `lib/core/floor/**` is now FROZEN.** TASK-023 owns only `test/simulation/**` so it cannot fix any of these — they need an ORCH-created successor task. (1) `ArbiterGrant.preempted` is **computed and never read** — `_arbitrate` ignores it, so an arbiter that pre-empts *itself* keeps transmitting until the new speaker's TX_START returns (~1.5 RTT) where a remote pre-empted holder stops at TX_GRANT (~0.5 RTT). Harmless on the synchronous loopback, a real double-transmit window on a live network; the fix is one line using the already-computed field. (2) **A pre-empted speaker gets no distinct effect** — it emits only `DispatchRadio(EndTransmit())`, so the user's transmission ends with no tone or haptic explaining why; spec-silent, but FR-025's "pre-emption tone on the channel" arguably wants one. (3) **`EmergencyPin` is a single slot** — a second EMG sender overwrites the first, and probe 3 confirmed that when the *second* sender clears, the first sender's still-active EMG indicator vanishes although it never cleared, violating FR-025's "until the sender clears it" for the first sender; it should be a set of pinning peers. (4) **`TX_GRANT` is unauthenticated and accepted unconditionally** — probe 5 injected a grant naming a peer that was not even in the roster, from a non-arbiter, and the live speaker stood down and installed the forged lease. This is **structurally unavoidable at this layer**: §8.6 L331's `TX_GRANT` carries only `peer` + `lease_ms` and has **no sender field**, so a receiver cannot tell the arbiter's grant from anyone else's. Spec gap, recorded as new follow-up (t); it matters most for TASK-023's byzantine/duplicate-arbiter cases. (5) **Roster is entirely injected** — `PRESENCE`/`RCHK`/`RCHK_ACK` are explicitly ignored and `updateRoster` is the only churn input, so §8.6's 5 s/3-miss departure detection lives in nobody's territory yet (TASK-020 owns presence); until then the ≤ 500 ms bound is only as good as whoever calls `updateRoster`. (6) **`LoopbackHub`/`LoopbackEndpoint` are test doubles exported from the production barrel** `floor.dart` — the same leak flagged on TASK-019's `FakeNsdPlatform` and TASK-008's `InMemorySettingsStore`, now three for three; TASK-023 genuinely needs `LoopbackHub`, so this one is arguably load-bearing, but it should move to a `testing/` sub-barrel. (7) **TOT bounds 30–120 s are now duplicated across two frozen territories** — `FloorEngine.minTot/maxTot` and TASK-008's `KeryxSettings` validator, with no shared constant and nothing wiring `busyLockout`/`totSeconds` from settings into the engine (both correctly out of territory); the integration task must not let them drift. Minor: a deny that arrives after a remote `TX_START` is swallowed without a `DenyBuzz` (the phase has already left `awaiting`), and `EmergencyPin.reset()` is never called by the engine.

**Positive worth recording for assignment heuristics: the TASK-006 disclosure instruction has fully landed.** GB volunteered all four of its spec-silent decisions (local lockout vs. arbiter BUSY, idempotent re-grant keeping remaining lease, `peer` as subject-not-sender, the 500 ms re-election reading) **in its Progress_Note before review** *and* in a dedicated "Decisions (spec-silent, pinned here)" README section — where TASK-019 disclosed one of four unprompted, this is four of four. That gap is now closed.

**UNLOCKS TASK-023** (floor-control simulation soak harness, KRX-044, `test/simulation/**`) — verified against the live PLAN.md: `Depends_On: TASK-022` is its sole prerequisite and is now `done`, so it is **fully eligible**. It is still `Assigned_To: TBD` and needs an owner. Whoever takes it inherits follow-ups (1), (3), (4) and (5) as the scenarios most likely to break the "zero double-grants" invariant, and gets a genuine head start: `VirtualClock` and `LoopbackHub` (with its `drop` hook for loss/partition and `inject` for byzantine messages) are already built and exercised. Branch `task/TASK-022-gb` deleted.
**Blocked_Reason:** —
**Updated_By:** ORCH
**Updated_At:** 2026-08-18T15:05:31Z

Whole file: 350 passed / 155 failed. **Root cause confirmed at SPEC level: this is a §8.6 gap, not merely an implementation bug.** ORCH read the §8.6 message table at source (spec L328–336): the ten types are `TX_REQ`/`TX_GRANT`/`TX_DENY`, `TX_START`/`TX_END`, `PRESENCE(peer, cs, seq)`, `RCHK`/`RCHK_ACK(peer, quality)`, `EMG`/`EMG_CLR`. **No message carries who currently holds the floor** — `RCHK_ACK`'s `quality` is the FR-066 S-meter, not floor state, and `PRESENCE` has no holder field. A peer joining mid-transmission therefore has NO protocol mechanism to learn a lease is live: it never received the one-shot `TX_START`, so `Arbiter.decide` sees `holder: null` and grants. Election being "the sole input… lexicographic minimum" (§8.6 normative) means that same blind joiner can also out-rank the incumbent and become arbiter. **S5's process was exemplary and is recorded here as the standard.** It hit 233/500 failures and correctly root-caused them to its OWN harness bug (`SafetyMonitor` never forgot silently-crashed peers, so a later unrelated grant was flagged against a long-dead peer), fixed it; hit 154/500 and found a SECOND harness issue (roster propagation modelled as an independent 0–400 ms draw rather than riding the transport), fixed it; then at 155/500 STOPPED assuming harness bugs and traced a full seed with instrumentation. Both commits `[TASK-023]`-tagged, PLAN.md discipline clean.

### TASK-024
**Title:** LINKED integration: livekit_client join/publish/subscribe mirroring PTT (KRX-052 + KRX-055)
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.4, §8.5, FR-043, FR-045, §11 E6 (KRX-052, KRX-055); lib/services/mesh/floor_data_channel_transport.dart (TASK-021's `FloorTransport` adapter over WebRTC — this task's LiveKit data-message adapter is the SAME pattern over a different transport; read it before writing code, do not invent a parallel abstraction); lib/core/rooms/** (TASK-007, deriveKeyed's isolate constraint)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/services/linked/**, test/services/linked/**
**Depends_On:** TASK-003, TASK-006, TASK-007
**Description:** Under `lib/services/linked/`: LINKED-path client — derive roomId (TASK-007), fetch JWT from the token service (TASK-003 contract), join the LiveKit room, pre-publish the muted audio track, mirror PTT state on it (same `TransmitGranted`/`EndTransmit`-driven `enabled` flip TASK-021 established for the LOCAL mesh — this is the second, not the first, implementation of that pattern), ride floor-control messages over LiveKit data messages using TASK-006's `FloorCodec` — **a second concrete `FloorTransport` implementation (`lib/core/floor/transport.dart`), this time wrapping LiveKit's data-message API instead of a WebRTC `RTCDataChannel`, mirroring TASK-021's `MeshFloorTransport` shape exactly** — and implement link-loss/regain behaviour: `NO LINK` flag, link_lost/link_up chirp events, auto-fallback to LOCAL, never a modal. **`deriveKeyed` (TASK-007's scrypt-backed keyed-channel derivation) is ~1-3s of pure-Dart computation and MUST run off the UI isolate (`compute()`/`Isolate.run()`) — this was flagged as mandatory before this task in TASK-007's own review and is now a hard acceptance criterion, not a note.** Tested against a LiveKit client abstraction; live-relay smoke test documented for review.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**

And `link_monitor_test.dart:51-61` adds a named FR-045 regression guard asserting the exact list `[LinkDegraded(), LinkResolved(useLocalFallback: true)]` for the null-reconnect case. **The two pre-existing LinkMonitor tests that were modified were NOT weakened** — they now supply `reconnect: () async => room` purely so the new give-up-on-null branch does not fire, which preserves exactly what each was originally testing (SDK-internal reconnecting-to-connected, and duplicate-dispatch suppression); the disclosure comment is accurate.

S5 verified against the livekit_client 2.11.0 SDK source that `publishAudioTrack` signals `muted: track.muted` in its initial request, so pre-muting is the SDK-native pattern rather than a workaround — that check was not asked for and is the right instinct. **Unprompted bonus fix:** `await track.stop()` on the no-local-participant error path, which previously leaked a live capture track on throw. The `enabled` setter (`:125-146`) now serializes through an await-chained `_pending` tail, so a fast key-up/key-down pair can no longer complete out of order and strand the mic unmuted after PTT release, and failures are now logged rather than silently swallowed by `unawaited`.

**NEW NON-BLOCKING FINDING (jj) — MANDATORY CONSTRAINT ON THE HOST-WIRING SUCCESSOR TASK, recorded because it is latent today and will not be obvious later.** `_reconnectRoom` replaces `_floorTransport` with a NEW `LinkedFloorTransport` and disposes the old one (closing its `incoming` `StreamController`). This is deliberate and pinned by test (`linked_controller_test.dart:290` asserts `isNot(same(firstTransport))`), not accidental. But the ratified design has the HOST wire `controller.floorTransport` into a `FloorEngine` at join time — so after any successful reconnect, that engine holds a disposed transport and LINKED floor control dies silently, meaning PTT arbitration would break after a transient blip. **It is genuinely latent right now:** an ORCH grep confirms nothing in `lib/` consumes `LinkedController.floorTransport` (the only `lib/` hits are `MeshFloorTransport`, a different class); the sole consumers are this task's own tests, and host-level wiring is explicitly out of this task's scope and does not exist yet. Not charged, and NOT to be re-raised against this branch. **Whoever builds the host wiring MUST handle transport replacement** — either a stable proxy `FloorTransport` whose underlying room swaps beneath it, or a `Stream<FloorTransport>` the host re-subscribes to on reconnect. This is the second constraint that task now inherits, alongside the SFX-projection wiring.

**MINOR, recorded not charged:** (i) `_join`'s disposed-unwind path returns normally after disconnecting, so a caller awaiting `joinNumbered()` observes success with `isJoined == false` — throwing `StateError` (as the entry guard does) would be more honest. (ii) `linkMonitorInitialBackoff`/`maxBackoff`/`maxAttempts` are constructor params on a production class existing only so tests can drive the give-up path without real backoff delays — pragmatic, documented, defaults match `LinkMonitor`'s own, accepted. (iii) `_applyMuteState` logs and swallows SDK failures, so a failed `mute()` leaves `_enabled == false` while the track is really live, with no retry — the dangerous ordering bug is fixed, this residual is a lesser one. (iv) `_enabled` is still assigned optimistically before the chain applies; correct-by-design (it is the *requested* gate state and PTT wants immediate feedback) now that ordering is guaranteed. (v) cosmetic: the give-up log reads "giving up after 0 attempts" in the null-reconnect case.

**Review_Findings:** REWORK (2026-08-20T22:55Z, ORCH on claude-opus-5). **S5's first rework on this project, and the surrounding work is strong — territory, PLAN.md discipline, Test_Evidence honesty and two of the three flagged concerns all came back clean.** Fix the two BLOCKING items on the same branch (`task/TASK-024-s5`, do NOT re-claim or re-branch), address the non-blocking ones where cheap, then return to `needs_review`.

**BLOCKING (1) — FR-045 / KRX-055 auto-fallback to LOCAL is unreachable in production. AC #5 is ticked `[x]` on behaviour that exists only under test injection.** `linked_controller.dart:140` constructs `LinkMonitor(room: room, dispatch: _dispatch)` with **no `reconnect:` argument**, so `_reconnect` is `null` on every production path. `link_monitor.dart:91` reads `if (_disposed || _reconnect == null) return;` — and that early return sits **before** the give-up branch at `:92-96` that dispatches `LinkResolved(useLocalFallback: true)`. Net production behaviour on a relay drop: `_onLost()` dispatches `LinkDegraded` (so `NO LINK` lights correctly), then `_scheduleReconnect()` returns immediately — nothing ever retries, and the reducer branch at `lib/core/state/radio_state.dart:421` that actually flips `mode` to `RadioMode.local` is never reached. The radio sits on `NO LINK` forever. FR-045 (spec L137) is a **Must**: "relay unreachable → radio drops to LOCAL with an audible 'link lost' double-chirp and `NO LINK` display flag; never a modal error dialog", and §11 E6 KRX-055 (L462) names "auto-fallback to LOCAL" as this task's own deliverable. **Why the green suite did not catch it — the wiring, not the unit, is untested:** `reconnect:` appears **only** at `link_monitor_test.dart:59` and `:81`, never in `lib/`; the fallback assertion at `link_monitor_test.dart:72` therefore only ever runs against an injected callback; and the one controller-level link-loss test (`linked_controller_test.dart:206`) asserts `LinkDegraded` (`:213`) and stops there — i.e. it covers exactly the half that does work. **Fix:** have `LinkedController._join` pass a real `reconnect` callback (re-mint the token and re-`connect`, since a LiveKit JWT is short-lived — reusing the stale `tokenResponse.token` will fail on a long outage), AND reorder `_scheduleReconnect` so the `_attempt >= _maxAttempts` give-up/fallback branch is evaluated **before** the `_reconnect == null` bail, so a null-reconnect monitor still degrades to LOCAL rather than hanging. Add a `LinkedController`-level test that drives a real disconnect through to `LinkResolved(useLocalFallback: true)` — the missing test is the actual defect here, the null argument is just how it surfaced.

**BLOCKING (2) — TS §8.5 violated: the mic is live and publishing before it is muted (hot-mic window on every join).** `livekit_client_adapter.dart:57-68` does `lk.LocalAudioTrack.create()` (`:58`, capture starts live) → `participant.publishAudioTrack(track)` (`:63`) → **then** `await track.mute()` (`:66`). Between `:63` and `:66` the microphone is genuinely publishing to the SFU. Spec L311 is explicit: "the audio track is **pre-published muted** on channel join; PTT grant flips `enabled=true`" — muted is a precondition of publishing, not a follow-up call. On a PTT radio whose entire premise is that the mic is closed until the user keys up, this is a privacy defect, not a style nit. **Fix:** `await track.mute()` before `publishAudioTrack(track)`. **Same file, same class of bug — `_LiveKitClientLocalAudioTrack.enabled` (`:108-117`) fires `unawaited(_track.unmute())` / `unawaited(_track.mute())` with no sequencing or queue.** A fast key-up/key-down pair issues `mute()` then `unmute()` concurrently; if they complete out of order the track is left **unmuted after PTT release** — a stuck-open mic, the worst failure mode this product has. Serialize the transitions (await-chain them off a single `Future` tail, or drop an in-flight op when superseded). Related: `_enabled` is assigned optimistically at `:111` before the async call resolves, so `LinkedController.isLocalTrackEnabled` can report a gate state the track does not actually have. **Note on scope:** `livekit_client_adapter.dart` being outside the unit suite is an accepted project convention (same as `rtc_adapter_flutter_webrtc.dart`) and is NOT being charged — but "untested by convention" does not cover an ordering error visible on inspection, and both of these are visible on inspection.

**NON-BLOCKING (a) — c8b9872 preflight is absent AND is misreported as present.** No `preflight_paths.py` output, `ls`, or `find` on `Owned_Paths` appears anywhere in the PLAN.md edits or the branch's commit messages (grep: 0 hits in both). The claim note records a *precedent survey* (reading `MeshFloorTransport`/`MeshController`/`FloorTransport`/`RtcAdapter` etc.) — genuine, valuable diligence, but a different check: that reads the files being mirrored, not the territory being written. The needs_review note then states "Preflight already recorded above (new territory)", which is not accurate — nothing was recorded above. Flagged, not charged as the rework driver (BLOCKING (1) already is), but noted because REVIEW.md records S5 pasting this **verbatim on TASK-027** on its first try, so this is a regression from S5's own established standard, and the inaccurate self-report is the part that matters more than the missing command. Re-run it and paste it verbatim on resubmission.

**NON-BLOCKING (b) — the off-isolate proof test is vacuous, but this is ORCH's drafting debt, NOT S5's error.** `linked_controller_test.dart:85-102` asserts `['synchronous-continuation', 'joinKeyed']`; because `joinKeyed` is `async`, that ordering holds identically whether `deriveKeyed` ran on an isolate or inline, so the test cannot fail on the condition it names. **AC #7 nevertheless PASSES on the implementation** — `linked_controller.dart:98` is `await compute(_deriveKeyedOffUiIsolate, passphrase)` with a correct top-level entry point at `:183`, which is exactly what the criterion mandates. S5 is not charged for this: the AC as ORCH wrote it explicitly offered "asserting it returns a `Future` that completes after other synchronous work" as an acceptable method, and S5 implemented that instruction literally and even documented the caveat in the test's own comment. **ORCH owns the fix** — the criterion's suggested method is unsound and must not be reused verbatim on TASK-025, which carries the identical AC. A sound form asserts the mechanism (e.g. that `deriveKeyed` is never invoked on the main isolate) rather than an ordering that `async` alone guarantees.

**NON-BLOCKING (c) — `_join` does not re-check `_disposed` after its awaits, leaking a live room.** `linked_controller.dart:122` checks `_disposed` once, then awaits at `:123`, `:128`, `:131`, `:136`. If `dispose()` lands during any of those, `_join` still resumes and assigns `_room`/`_localTrack`/`_floorTransport`/`_linkMonitor` (`:137-140`) onto a disposed controller — a room that is now never disconnected, holding a published audio track. Re-check `_disposed` after the awaits and unwind (disconnect the room) if it flipped.

**NON-BLOCKING (d) — `TokenClient` has no timeout, which is itself an FR-045 gap.** `token_client.dart` sets neither `HttpClient.connectionTimeout` nor a per-request timeout, so a token service that accepts the connection and then hangs blocks the join indefinitely with no `TokenTransportException` and no path to LOCAL fallback. "Relay unreachable" (FR-045) includes "reachable but wedged"; the current code only degrades on outright connection failure. Add a bounded timeout mapping to `TokenTransportException`.

**NON-BLOCKING (e) — `_baseUrl.resolve('/token')` (`token_client.dart:38`) discards any path prefix** on the configured base URL (`https://host/api` → `https://host/token`). Fine for the current deployment, wrong if the token service is ever mounted behind a path prefix. Prefer `resolve('token')` against a trailing-slash base, or join explicitly.

**RATIFIED, NOT A DEFECT — the link_lost/link_up chirp deferral is spec-correct and S5 was right to escalate it rather than silently scope down.** TS §8.2 (L289) states "UI, audio, haptics, and network are all projections of it [the reducer]", so a `LinkMonitor` that dispatches `LinkDegraded`/`LinkResolved` and plays no audio itself is the architecturally correct shape; playing a chirp from the transport layer would have violated §8.2. The chirp genuinely cannot be delivered from this territory because **no SFX player is wired to any `RadioState` transition anywhere in the app yet** — the same gap the 2026-08-20 handover records for `FaceScreen`, now blocking sound in a second place. `SfxId.linkLost`/`linkUp` (TASK-010/011) exist and are ready. **This is ORCH sequencing debt, owed a successor task; it is not TASK-024's to fix and must not be re-raised against this branch.** AC #5 still fails on BLOCKING (1) — the auto-fallback half — not on the chirp half.

**CLEAN — verified, not taken on trust.** Territory: 13 files, 1408 insertions / 0 deletions, every path inside `lib/services/linked/**` + `test/services/linked/**`; the exclusion diff is empty. ### TASK-025
**Title:** Event QR generate/scan + keryx:// deep links (KRX-054)
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** medium
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md FR-043, FR-044, §11 E6 (KRX-054); lib/core/rooms/** (TASK-007, deriveKeyed's isolate constraint)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/features/event_qr/**, test/features/event_qr/**, dossiers/TASK-025.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-007
**Description:** Under `lib/features/event_qr/`: export any channel as a QR + `keryx://` deep link encoding region, channel, code (or keyed-channel token) and expiry, with expiry presets 4 h / 24 h (default) / 7 d / no-expiry (extra explicit tap); scanner flow that tunes the radio instantly on scan; deep-link intent handling payload parser (Android manifest registration itself lands with the android-chain tasks — parser and UI here). Token format versioned; unit tests for encode/decode/expiry. **A keyed-channel token calls `deriveKeyed` (TASK-007's scrypt-backed derivation, ~1-3s of pure-Dart computation) — MUST run off the UI isolate (`compute()`/`Isolate.run()`), same mandatory constraint as TASK-024, now a hard acceptance criterion here too.**
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] "any channel can be exported as a QR code + keryx:// deep link encoding region, channel, code (or keyed-channel token), and expiry" per FR-044
- [x] "Default expiry: 24 h, with presets (4 h 'session', 24 h, 7 d, no expiry — the last requiring an explicit extra tap)" per FR-044
- [x] "Scanning tunes the radio instantly" per FR-044 — scan result emits a tuning intent
- [x] Event QR is a first-class LINKED join method per FR-043
- [x] `deriveKeyed` runs off the UI isolate for any keyed-channel QR/link generation or scan — *ORCH-authored, MANDATORY per TASK-007 follow-up (dd)*. **Two things must both hold, and the test must be capable of FAILING if the call were made inline.** (1) **Mechanism:** the production path invokes `deriveKeyed` through `compute()` / `Isolate.run()` with a top-level or `static` entry point (a closure will not marshal). (2) **Behaviour — the main isolate must be provably NOT blocked while the derivation is in flight:** e.g. a `Timer.periodic` (or repeatedly-incremented counter driven by the event loop) started before the call must be observed to have ticked at least once before the returned `Future` completes. This is the property that matters and it cannot be faked: `deriveKeyed` is ~1–3 s of **synchronous** pure-Dart scrypt, so running it inline starves the event loop and no timer can fire. **Do NOT use the form "assert a Future completes after subsequent synchronous work" — that was TASK-024's criterion and it is UNSOUND:** any `async` function satisfies it whether or not the work was offloaded, so it passes identically on a broken implementation (see TASK-024 Review_Findings finding (b))
- [x] Encode/decode/expiry unit tests green
**Branch:** task/TASK-025-s5

**CRITERIA 1–6, ALL VERIFIED AT SOURCE.**
1. **FR-103** — `RadioForegroundService.kt` implements the persistent notification with channel/PTT-action/power-off action; manifest declares `android:name=".RadioForegroundService"`, `android:stopWithTask="false"` (radio survives a recents-swipe, matching the note "Power-off is an explicit action, not recents-swipe" at `onTaskRemoved`).
2. **§8.8 service types + wake lock** — manifest `android:foregroundServiceType="mediaPlayback|microphone"` (spec L365 exact match, verified via independent extraction); wake lock is `PowerManager.PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK`, acquired only on `PHASE_RX`/`PHASE_TX`, released on `PHASE_IDLE` and in `teardownLocks()` — read at source, not summary.
3. **Audio focus "transient-may-duck for RX; abandon on power-off"** — `requestRxFocus()` uses `AUDIOFOCUS_GAIN_TRANSIENT_MAY_DUCK`, requested only on RX. **ORCH specifically investigated a suspected deviation and ruled it a non-issue:** focus is abandoned on every RX→IDLE transition, not only at explicit power-off (`handleStop`'s `teardownLocks()` also abandons it, so power-off is covered too). Read against Android's own audio-focus contract, request/release scoped to the active RX burst is the textbook-correct use of a TRANSIENT grant — holding it continuously through standby (when FR-102 says "no media flows") would be the actual anti-pattern. Not a defect; the code is more correct than a literal reading of the spec sentence would require.
4. **FR-102 standby / no polling** — `test('start does not schedule periodic work', ...)` at `radio_service_controller_test.dart:89` is a dedicated regression guard for exactly this criterion; `handleStart`/`handleStop`/`applyPhase` are event-driven with no `Timer.periodic` anywhere in the Kotlin or Dart source (ORCH grep confirms zero periodic-timer constructs in either).
5. **OEM-kill detection** — `onDestroy()` sets `store.killedDirty = true` and emits `EVENT_KILLED` only when `!userRequestedStop && store.poweredOn`; `killedDirty` is picked back up on the next `handleStart` so a relaunch after an OEM kill still surfaces the event. Three dedicated controller tests cover it (`OEM kill without user stop emits serviceKilled`, `start after a dirty kill flag emits serviceKilled`, `user stop does not emit serviceKilled` — the negative case, proving the heuristic doesn't false-positive on a normal stop).
6. **Android 14+ PTT gating** — `pttActionPermitted(): Boolean = Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= 34` (34 = Android 14, correct); plumbed end-to-end through `RESULT_PTT_ENABLED` into the Dart facade's `pttActionEnabled` getter so a host can adapt UI when the action isn't offered — not just gated silently on the native side.

**MANIFEST — all six declared permissions verified present** (`FOREGROUND_SERVICE`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE`, `WAKE_LOCK`, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, `RECORD_AUDIO`), with a comment correctly scoping runtime `RECORD_AUDIO`/`POST_NOTIFICATIONS` grants to FR-104 (the first-run flow), not this task — right call, right disclosure.

**CALIBRATION NOTE ON THE SELF-REPORTED "MUTATION" — not a defect, a precision correction for the record.** GB's Test_Evidence describes a mutation check (`PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK` → `SCREEN_BRIGHT_WAKE_LOCK` turning the native-contract suite red). ORCH read `radio_service_native_contract_test.dart` at source: this suite is a **static source-text scan** (`File(...).readAsStringSync()` + `expect(text, contains('TOKEN'))`) over the Kotlin/manifest files, not a runtime/behavioral exercise of the Android service — appropriate and consistent with this project's standing convention that native adapters (LiveKit, WebRTC) are analyzed-not-executed under `flutter test`. Changing a literal string in the source obviously fails a `contains()` assertion; that is real regression protection (catches accidental deletion/renaming of the load-bearing tokens) but is a materially weaker guarantee than TASK-030's genuine behavioral mutation (which proved a runtime code path, not a source string, was exercised). GB's claim is accurate as stated — it did not overclaim what the test proves — ORCH is only calibrating what the evidence is worth for future comparison.

**INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED, NOT TAKEN ON TRUST.** Branch `636b560`: `flutter analyze` "No issues found!" exit 0; scoped `test/services/platform` **22/22** (static `grep -c "test("`: 15 + 7 = 22, exact match, no reporter artifact); FULL unfiltered suite **449/449 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, matching GB's claim exactly. **`flutter build apk --debug` independently re-run and SUCCEEDED** — `✓ Built build\app\outputs\flutter-apk\app-debug.apk`, 211,503,318 bytes confirmed on disk — proving the Kotlin genuinely compiles against the real Android toolchain, not just `flutter analyze`'s Dart-side view. Merged tree `431a61c` re-verified by ORCH directly: **449/449, analyze clean.**

**SCOPE, DISCLOSED HONESTLY.** GB's own Progress_Note records a first Kotlin build failure (`Intent.apply` reassigning a `val flags`) fixed before handoff — disclosed rather than silently hidden behind the final green build.

### TASK-027
**Title:** RadioState projection fields + floor→reducer bridge (state successor, follow-up (m))
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.2, §8.4, §8.6, §8.9, FR-025, FR-045, FR-065, FR-067, FR-069, §9 NFR-10; specs/KERYX_UI_Design_Specification_v1.0.md §6 (State Catalogue, KRX-018)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/core/state/**, test/core/state/**
**Depends_On:** TASK-004, TASK-022
**Description:** Reopen the frozen `lib/core/state/**` territory (the 010→011 successor pattern) to close the gap that blocks TASK-017. TASK-004's `RadioState` exposes only phase/mode/channel/code; DS §6's State Catalogue and TS §8.2 ("One reducer owns this. UI, audio, haptics, and network are all projections of it") require the reducer to also expose the telltale flags (NO LINK/EMG/PRV/REPLAY/MON/SCAN/VOX), the station count (`STN n`, FR-067), the active-speaker identity (§6.1's active-speaker line; DS §6 "RX active (callsign shown)"), and the aggregate S-meter/quality (S1–S9, FR-069 / §8.9). None of these exist today. TASK-022's floor engine currently holds EMG / active-speaker(`holder`) / arbiter as **floor-local state + effects** (`EmgPinned`/`EmgCleared`/`ArbiterChanged` effects and getters) routed *around* the frozen reducer, and station count has **no source at all** (`_peers` has no public getter). This task extends `RadioState` with those fields plus the reducer events/transitions that set them, AND defines a documented one-way bridge so the floor engine and presence/telemetry feeds populate them — so TASK-017 renders ONE state source (`RadioState`), not two.
**BRIDGE DESIGN DECISION (ORCH-proposed, ratifiable at review).** Of the two shapes — (A) new reducer events fed by the floor engine, vs (B) a separate projection/view-model layer that merges `RadioState` + `FloorEngine` getters — ORCH proposes **(A): extend the sealed `RadioEvent` hierarchy with new events (e.g. `EmergencyPinned`/`EmergencyCleared`, `ActiveSpeakerChanged`, `RosterUpdated(stationCount)`, `SignalQualityUpdated(sMeter)`, plus telltale toggles for MON/SCAN/VOX/REPLAY/PRV/NO-LINK), and add a thin one-way bridge/adapter in this territory that subscribes to TASK-022's existing sealed effect stream and translates each floor effect into the corresponding reducer event.** Rationale: (B) would cement two-source rendering and contradict TS §8.2 L289's single-reducer invariant (and would formalise the `_Phase`-vs-`RadioPhase` drift risk TASK-022's review flagged); (A) collapses rendering back to the one reducer. The pattern is already established — the floor engine already emits `DispatchRadio(RadioEvent)` effects the host feeds to the reducer, so this only extends that ingress to its non-reducer effects. The bridge is strictly one-way (floor effects → reducer events → `RadioState`; the reducer never calls back into the engine). Station count and per-net S-meter originate from the **presence/telemetry feeds** (TASK-020 roster / KRX-035 telemetry→S-meter), not the frozen floor engine; the bridge is the single documented ingress for those too. The exact field/event split is proposed here and ratified at review.
**SECONDARY (scope tightly — do NOT let these bloat the blocker):** fold in TASK-004 Review_Findings' "fix opportunistically while the territory is live" items — a `PowerOff` event/edge (FR-065 replay-clear on radio-off); split the Riverpod host into its own file so a purity test can assert the reducer imports zero framework (follow-up 4); migrate `StateNotifier`→`Notifier` (follow-up 5); rename `LinkRecovered`'s fallback branch (follow-up 6); gate `LinkDegraded` to powered-on phases (follow-up 7); gate `SetMode` to floor-idle per TS §8.4 "at floor-idle only" (follow-up 8).
**tuneDelta — SPECIAL CASE, do NOT depend on an unruled decision:** `tuneDelta` + channel/code wrap-vs-clamp (TASK-004 review item 2 + follow-up (k)) is BLOCKED on the unresolved ORCH spec ruling for wrap-vs-clamp at 99/1 and 38/00. Add `tuneDelta` HERE **only if (k) is ruled before this task dispatches**; otherwise defer it to a follow-on. This task's `Depends_On` deliberately does NOT include (k) or any unruled decision.
**Coverage:** keep TASK-004's ratified 100%-branch discipline via the table-driven `RadioPhase` × event matrix, extended to the new events; state evidence as the matrix (count of legal edges asserted + count of illegal pairs asserted no-op) plus `LF/LH`, NOT a `--branch-coverage` percentage — the toolchain's LCOV emits no `BRF/BRH` decision denominator here (documented in TASK-004's rework), so no percentage is claimable.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] `RadioState` exposes the DS §6 telltale set (NO LINK, EMG, PRV, REPLAY, MON, SCAN, VOX) as projection fields, per DS §6 State Catalogue (KRX-018) and TS §8.2 ("UI, audio, haptics, and network are all projections of it")
- [x] `RadioState` carries a station-count field, per FR-067 ("Presence: `STN n` count on the display")
- [x] `RadioState` carries an active-speaker identity field (nullable), per §6.1's active-speaker line and DS §6 ("RX active (callsign shown)")
- [x] `RadioState` carries an aggregate S-meter/quality field (S1–S9), per FR-069 ("the status-strip meter is aggregate … Signal meter (S1–S9) per §8.9")
- [x] New reducer events set each new field, added to the sealed `RadioEvent` hierarchy with exhaustive-switch handling preserved so a future subclass is a compile error — *ORCH-authored engineering criterion* preserving TASK-004's ratified single-reducer invariant (TS §8.2)
- [x] A documented one-way bridge feeds TASK-022's floor effects (EMG / active-speaker / arbiter) and the presence/telemetry roster into those events so TASK-017 reads ONE source — *ORCH-authored, ratifiable*: the bridge shape (proposal (A) above) is the design decision reviewed here, grounded in TS §8.2 L289 and TASK-022 Review_Findings (m)
- [x] Secondary items landed and scoped as secondary: `PowerOff` edge, Riverpod-host split + reducer purity test, `Notifier` migration, `LinkRecovered` fallback rename, `LinkDegraded` gated to powered-on phases, `SetMode` gated to floor-idle (TS §8.4 "at floor-idle only") — *ORCH-authored engineering criteria from TASK-004 Review_Findings*
- [x] `tuneDelta` added only if follow-up (k) is ruled before dispatch, else deferred; this task does not depend on (k) — *ORCH-authored, conditional (TASK-004 follow-up 2 + follow-up (k))*
- [x] 100%-branch discipline held via the table-driven `RadioPhase` × event matrix (TASK-004 pattern), evidence stated as the matrix + `LF/LH`, no `--branch-coverage` percentage claimed, per NFR-10 ("Reducer/state machine 100% branch") and TASK-004's documented LCOV limitation

- **RATIFIED — the bridge design.** Proposal **(A) is ADOPTED as built.** CX built (A), not (B): there is no view-model and no merge layer. `RadioStateBridge` subscribes to `FloorEngine.effects`, translates `EmgPinned`/`EmgCleared`/`ArbiterChanged` into `EmergencyPinned`/`EmergencyCleared`/`ArbiterIdentityChanged`, forwards existing `DispatchRadio` events, and exposes `updateRoster`/`updateSignalQuality`/`updatePrivateChannel`/`updateMonitor`/`updateScan`/`updateVox`/`updateReplay` as the single documented ingress for the presence (TASK-020) and telemetry (KRX-035) feeds. **The bridge is genuinely ONE-WAY and this is proven structurally, not by assertion:** `radio_state.dart` has ZERO import statements of any kind, so the reducer cannot reference the bridge, the engine, or Riverpod even in principle; the bridge only ever calls `_dispatch(...)` and reads two public getters (`engine.holder`, `engine.localPeerId`); it never mutates a `RadioState` and never calls back into the engine. Station count is sourced coherently and exactly as the block prescribed — TASK-022's frozen engine still has no `_peers` getter, so `stationCount` comes from the presence feed via `bridge.updateRoster(int)` rather than from the engine. **Deviation from (A), and it is an improvement:** CX added `ArbiterIdentityChanged`/`RadioState.arbiterId`, which the proposal's field list did not enumerate — but criterion 6 names the arbiter effect explicitly, so it is in-scope, and without it `ArbiterChanged` would have been a floor effect with nowhere to land. Second deviation, minor and defensible: `_projectFloorEvent` derives the active speaker by reading `engine.holder` at effect-delivery time rather than from an effect payload, because TASK-022 puts no holder in the payload; the exhaustive `switch` over `RadioEvent` inside the bridge is a nice touch that makes a future event a compile error in TWO places, not one.

This is the cleanest scoped rework on the project's record. **RENAME COMPLETE AND CONSISTENT.** `git grep` for `callsign` across `lib/core/state/**` + `test/core/state/**` on the branch returns only the two dartdoc prose lines that deliberately explain the distinction - zero code references to the old parameter name remain. `ActiveSpeakerChanged(this.speaker)` / `final String? speaker;` with dartdoc stating it carries a **peerId**, that the bridge feeds it `FloorEngine.holder`/`localPeerId`, and that the peerId->callsign mapping is TASK-009's `displayNames` roster applied downstream by TASK-017 and must not happen here since the reducer has no roster to consult - exactly what instruction (3) asked for. The constructor is positional, so the three bridge call sites and all test call sites are unaffected; a repo-wide grep confirms `ActiveSpeakerChanged` appears only in `radio_state.dart`, `radio_state_bridge.dart`, the test file, PLAN.md and the dossier. No stale reference anywhere. **THE 8 RATIFIED CRITERIA REMAIN INTACT - NO REGRESSION.** Branch tree: `flutter analyze` clean, `test/core/state/` **18/18** (16 prior + 2 new), full suite **142/142** - matching S5's Test_Evidence number for number. MERGED tree: merge clean (ort, zero conflicts), `flutter analyze` clean, full suite **193/193**, zero failures. The phase x event matrix is unchanged at 123 legal / 85 illegal over 26 events x 8 phases = 208 pairs, correctly - S5 stated plainly that the two new tests are standalone negative cases *outside* the matrix rather than inflating the matrix count, which is the honest description. `LF 167 / LH 145` unchanged with the correct explanation (the guard lines were already reached on their true branch), and **no `--branch-coverage` percentage claimed** - the negative instruction from TASK-004's rework held under a second unit. **TERRITORY CLEAN.** 5 files, 529 insertions / 60 deletions, all inside `lib/core/state/**` + `test/core/state/**`; the pathspec-exclusion diff for everything outside `Owned_Paths` returns EMPTY. **PLAN.md DISCIPLINE CLEAN.** S5 correctly did NOT re-claim the task, did NOT create a new branch, and resumed on `task/TASK-027-s5` exactly as its dispatch instructed; its single PLAN.md commit `dbfdf1d` (+19/-4) touches only TASK-027's own block - no frontmatter, no other unit's block. c8b9872 preflight re-run in S5's own worktree and **pasted verbatim** as the raw tool block. **UNLOCKS NOTHING - stated precisely, verified against the live PLAN.md rather than assumed:** a grep for `Depends_On:.*027` returns EMPTY; no task in the plan carries TASK-027 as a dependency. In particular TASK-017's `Depends_On` is TASK-004/012/013/014/015/016, NOT TASK-027 - so this task removes the *substantive* blocker follow-up (m) recorded against TASK-017 (the frozen `lib/core/state/**` lacking the DS section 6 fields) without changing any dependency edge. `lib/core/state/**` is now frozen again; the items below need a successor task on the 010->011 pattern.
- **NON-BLOCKING, new this round - no action from S5.** (dd) **The two new negative tests are shadowed by the constructor's own asserts.** Under the mutation both tests go red via `RadioState`'s `assert(stationCount >= 0)` / `assert(signalQuality >= min && <= max)` firing inside `copyWith`, not via an `expect` value mismatch. The tests are therefore correct and do their job in a debug/test build - deleting the reducer guard IS caught - but they cannot distinguish 'reducer guard absent' from 'constructor assert absent', and in an assert-stripped release build they would not fail this way at all. This is the mirror image of the TASK-008 finding charged against CX (a guard dead behind an earlier assert); here it is the *test* that is shadowed. Not charged: S5 executed instructions (1)/(2) exactly as written and the instruction did not ask it to pin the throwing layer. Carry the sharper form into the successor task: **assert which layer rejected, not merely that the state was unchanged.** (ee) The pre-existing fixtures still use `ActiveSpeakerChanged('BRAVO-7')` - a callsign-shaped literal - in tests S5 was correctly told not to touch, which now reads slightly against the new dartdoc. Cosmetic; fix when the territory next opens. (ff) All twelve prior non-blocking items (w)-(cc) from the previous round remain open and unaddressed **by design** - S5 was told to leave them alone and did. They still need their successor task before TASK-017, especially (w) (TotWarn/TotCut/TransmitDenied have no `RadioState` field, so DS section 6's 'TX time-out warning', 'TX denied/busy' and 'LAN?' face states are still unrenderable from the single source) and (x)/(y). (gg) **Process note for ORCH, not a defect:** S5's dossier work log records that on first reading PLAN.md it found a live uncommitted GB edit (TASK-007's block) in the shared main checkout, and per `plan_guard.py`'s refusal and the documented recovery path it ran `git checkout -- PLAN.md` - scoped to PLAN.md only - then reapplied its own block against a fresh read. **No work was lost** (GB's TASK-007 block was subsequently committed as `a1dbc78`, reviewed and merged as `e4078e6`, and is intact in master - verified), and S5 disclosed the action unprompted, which is exactly right. But a builder discarding another builder's uncommitted PLAN.md working copy is a real concurrency hazard that happened to be harmless this once; the documented recovery path should be tightened to 'stash, don't discard', or `plan_commit.sh` should hold a lock. Raise before the next two-builder wave.

TASK-005 tokenized only PT's `:root` variables; TASK-012 then had to HARDCODE the glass-frame chrome (`Color(0xFF0A0F0C)` border + `Color.fromRGBO(0,0,0,0.85)` inner shadow + `Color.fromRGBO(255,255,255,0.05)` highlight + `Colors.transparent` bloom stop) because those are PT `.glass` L51-58 *inline* values the prototype never abstracts into `:root`, so the frozen theme exposes no token. ### TASK-029
**Title:** RadioState TX time-out/denied fields (state successor #2, closes follow-up (w))
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_UI_Design_Specification_v1.0.md §6 (State Catalogue, KRX-018); specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.6, FR-022, FR-023, FR-026
**Owned_Paths:** lib/core/state/**, test/core/state/**
**Depends_On:** TASK-022, TASK-027
**Description:** Reopen the frozen `lib/core/state/**` territory (the 010→011 / 005→028 successor pattern — see TASK-027's own Description and Review_Findings for the bridge's existing shape before touching anything) to close TASK-027 Review_Findings follow-up (w). TASK-022's floor engine already emits `TotWarn`/`TotCut` effects (FR-023: "Warning chirp at T-5 s, hard cut + penalty tone at 0, floor released") and a deny decision on busy-lockout (FR-022: "PTT press yields a denied buzz"), but TASK-027's bridge (`RadioStateBridge`, `lib/core/state/`) deliberately `break`s on `TotWarn`/`TotCut` in its `switch` — dropping them — and its handling of the deny path returns straight to `idle` leaving no displayable flag. Consequently DS §6's named face states "TX time-out warning" and "TX denied/busy" have no `RadioState` field, so TASK-017 (face assembly) would have to subscribe to TASK-022's floor-effect stream directly to render them — precisely the two-source rendering TASK-027's bridge design (A) exists to prevent (TS §8.2 L289's single-reducer invariant). This task extends the sealed `RadioEvent` hierarchy with the missing event(s) and adds the corresponding `RadioState` field(s), then extends the bridge's exhaustive switch to translate `TotWarn`→field-set, `TotCut`→field-clear (TX already ends here per FR-023, "floor released"), and the busy-lockout deny decision→a displayable transient flag, so all three read from the one reducer.
**FIELD/EVENT SHAPE — ORCH-proposed, ratifiable at review (same latitude TASK-027's bridge design was given).** Two reasonable shapes: (A) a persistent `isTotWarning` bool set true on `TotWarn`, cleared on `TotCut` or on the next event that ends TX (whichever the bridge already uses to mark TX end); plus a transient `isTransmitDenied`-style flag set true on the busy-lockout deny decision, cleared by the next state-changing `RadioEvent` (mirroring how `PttButtonState`'s own 260 ms deny-flash already works at the widget level in TASK-015 — this reducer field is the state-catalogue source of truth, not a replacement for that widget timing). (B) fold both into the existing telltale-flag pattern TASK-027 already established for NO LINK/EMG/PRV/REPLAY/MON/SCAN/VOX, if that reads more consistently against the existing `RadioState` shape. Pick whichever keeps the reducer pure (no internal timers — clearing must be event-driven, same discipline TASK-027 held) and disclose the choice, same as every prior successor task in this territory.
**EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE — do not add a `LAN?` field.** DS §6's state catalogue lists a third related state, `LAN?`, in the same line as "TX time-out warning"/"TX denied/busy", but per TS §8.3 L297 it is driven by the UDP broadcast discovery fallback ("if mDNS is filtered … a UDP broadcast beacon fallback runs … if both fail the display shows `LAN?` and the troubleshooting card") — KRX-033, a LAN-discovery feature that does not exist yet (TASK-020, LAN signaling, is still unassigned and gated on follow-up (s); KRX-033's fallback specifically has no task at all). Adding a `LAN?` field now would have no real signal to drive it and risks inventing behaviour ahead of its own spec-silent wire contract. TASK-017 will not render the `LAN?` state until KRX-033 lands — acceptable, matching TASK-027's own precedent (finding (z): "TASK-017 must not render telltales it can't source").
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] `RadioState` exposes a field reflecting TASK-022's `TotWarn` effect ("TX time-out warning" per DS §6), set/cleared by new sealed `RadioEvent`(s) with exhaustive-switch handling preserved
- [x] `RadioState` exposes a field reflecting the busy-lockout deny decision ("TX denied/busy" per DS §6 / FR-022), displayable without TASK-017 subscribing to the floor stream directly
- [x] The bridge's `switch` over `FloorEngine.effects` no longer `break`s on `TotWarn`/`TotCut` — both are translated into reducer events, per the ratified field/event shape above
- [x] No new field is cleared by an internal timer — every transition is event-driven, preserving the reducer's purity (TS §8.2 single-reducer invariant; same discipline as every existing `RadioState` field)
- [x] `LAN?` is explicitly NOT added — confirm via Progress_Note, not silently — *ORCH-authored, scope guard*
- [x] TASK-004/027's ratified 100%-branch discipline held via the table-driven `RadioPhase` × event matrix, extended to the new event(s); evidence stated as the matrix + `LF/LH`, no `--branch-coverage` percentage claimed (documented LCOV limitation, same as TASK-004/027)
- [x] Every existing TASK-027 field/event/test preserved — this task only ADDs; a mutation-style check (delete the new guard, confirm the suite goes red) recommended given TASK-027's own criterion-9 rework was exactly this class of gap

**Description:** Successor task reopening the frozen `lib/core/settings/**` territory on the 010→011 pattern, to fix defects TASK-008's review proved empirically and to add the keys its dependents cannot add from their own territories. **This task exists because TASK-018 is currently un-dispatchable without it** (its Description requires a dim-mode control that has no persisted key, and its criterion 6 would pass at the repository level while the panel rendered stale values). Six things: (1) make `load()` crash-safe — it currently catches only `FormatException`/`ArgumentError`, so a non-object JSON blob (`[]`, `5`, `"hello"`, `null`) throws an uncaught `_TypeError` from the `as Map` cast and a well-formed-but-out-of-range blob (`totSeconds: 999`) throws an uncaught `_AssertionError`, taking down `settingsProvider`, `save()` and `rememberChannel()` alike; (2) close the debug/release split — asserts are stripped in release, so the same corrupt blob that crashes debug silently yields `totSeconds: 999` in release, defeating FR-023's 30–120 bound on the build users actually run: **validate and clamp on READ, do not rely on constructor asserts**; (3) add the missing persisted keys — `dimMode` (FR-108, auto/manual), `mode` (FR-040, default AUTO, so the user's LOCAL/AUTO/LINKED choice survives restart) and VOX sensitivity/hang-time (FR-024, persist keys now, feature later); (4) make `settingsProvider` live — it is a one-shot `FutureProvider` that never invalidates, so every watcher holds the first-loaded value for the process lifetime; convert to an `AsyncNotifier` (or equivalent) that re-emits after `save()`/`rememberChannel()`; (5) implement the squelch unit contract ruled below; (6) serialize `rememberChannel()`'s unguarded read-modify-write so overlapping calls cannot lose an update (TS §6.2 caps the knob at 12 channel crossings/second, so bursts are real). **ORCH RULING — squelch unit (closes TASK-008 finding (5), do not re-litigate):** the persisted value STAYS `int 0–10` (already shipped, and discrete steps are the right user-facing model), and this task adds a documented normalized accessor returning `level / 10.0` as a `double` in `0.0..1.0` for `BedMixer.gainsFor(double)`, which is merged, frozen, and throws outside that range. ### TASK-031
**Title:** Floor-control late-joiner double-grant fix (PRESENCE holder/lease fields + join guard) — closes finding (ll)
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.6 (the "Late-joiner floor-state blind spot" ratification, 2026-08-21) and §8.6's `PRESENCE` row; PLAN.md TASK-023 finding (ll) (the deterministic repro and the three costed options — this task builds option A only); lib/core/protocol/timing.dart `FloorTiming.presenceHeartbeat` (the existing 5 s constant this task's guard is keyed to, do not invent a second one); test/simulation/soak_test.dart's "KNOWN ISSUE surfaced by this harness" group (TASK-023's minimal deterministic repro — read it before writing code, your fix must make it pass without weakening the harness)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/core/protocol/**, lib/core/floor/**, test/core/protocol/**, test/core/floor/**, dossiers/TASK-031.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-006, TASK-022
**Description:** Reopens two FROZEN territories (TASK-006, TASK-022) as a single task because the fix is genuinely one seam split across both: `lib/core/protocol/**` gains the two new optional `PRESENCE` fields (`holder`, `lease_remaining_ms`) in the `FloorCodec`/message model, and `lib/core/floor/**`'s `Arbiter`/`FloorEngine` gains the join-guard that reads them. Splitting this into two tasks would force one to build against the other's uncommitted field, so it stays one task, one reviewer, one merge. Implement exactly the ratified §8.6 amendment: (1) `PRESENCE` carries optional `holder` (peerId string or absent) and `lease_remaining_ms` (int, present iff `holder` is present) — encode/decode via the existing `FloorCodec`, both fields optional on the wire so old peers on this same protocol version still decode fine (no version bump, per §8.6's existing unknown-field/optional-field tolerance); the arbiter populates these on every outgoing `PRESENCE` from its own live view of the floor. (2) A peer MUST NOT self-grant on any `TX_REQ` — including the initial-request-becomes-self-grant path `Arbiter.decide` currently takes when `holder` is `null` — until it has either (a) observed at least one full `presenceHeartbeat` interval since joining the channel, or (b) direct proof the floor was idle before any peer joined (e.g. it was the first peer present). This must be a real elapsed-time/observation gate, not a message-count heuristic that a burst of early `PRESENCE`s could satisfy instantly — TASK-023's soak harness churns peers at realistic timing and will catch a heuristic that races ahead of a genuine heartbeat. (3) During the guard window, a `TX_REQ` from the local peer must resolve to `TX_DENY` (a real spec-defined outcome) rather than silently dropping the request or hanging — pick `BUSY`, since the peer genuinely does not yet know whether the floor is busy, and this is functionally the same uncertainty `BUSY` already covers. (4) The ≤ 50 ms TX-path (§8.5) and normal in-channel PTT after the guard window must be provably untouched — this is a join-time-only change.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] `PRESENCE` encodes/decodes optional `holder`/`lease_remaining_ms` per the §8.6 amendment; absent on both fields when idle, present together when not; old-format `PRESENCE` (missing both fields) still decodes cleanly — round-trip and backward-compat both unit tested
- [x] The arbiter/engine populates outgoing `PRESENCE.holder`/`lease_remaining_ms` from its own live floor state, not a stale or cached snapshot
- [x] A newly-joined peer cannot self-grant before observing one `FloorTiming.presenceHeartbeat` interval (5 s) since joining, UNLESS it has direct proof the floor was idle before any peer joined — both branches unit tested, including a real elapsed-time gate (not satisfiable by a burst of PRESENCE messages arriving faster than real time)
- [x] A `TX_REQ` from the local peer during the guard window resolves to `TX_DENY(BUSY)`, not silent drop or hang
- [ ] **TASK-023's exact deterministic repro (`soak_test.dart`'s "KNOWN ISSUE surfaced by this harness" group) now PASSES, unmodified** — do not edit `test/simulation/**`, it is outside this task's territory; if the repro still fails after your fix, the fix is wrong, not the test
- [x] Re-running TASK-023's full 500-seed soak (via a subagent, report pass/fail counts) shows zero double-grant assertions from the late-joiner class specifically (§8.6 timing-constant tests and other pre-existing invariants must stay green; if any other, unrelated failure class remains, name it explicitly rather than silently absorbing it into this criterion)
- [x] §8.5's ≤ 50 ms attack design and normal in-channel `TX_REQ`→`TX_GRANT` after the guard window are unmodified — a test proves a `TX_REQ` issued after the guard window has elapsed resolves with the SAME latency characteristics as before this change (no new await chain on the hot path)
- [x] Full `flutter test` suite green (not just `test/core/protocol/` or `test/core/floor/`) and `flutter analyze` clean
**Branch:** task/TASK-031-gb

3. **ROOT CAUSE OF THE RESIDUAL, TRACED TO SOURCE BY ORCH, NOT JUST ACCEPTED FROM GB'S DIAGNOSIS — though it matches GB's own diagnosis exactly.** `Arbiter.maySelfGrant` (`arbiter.dart:72-81`) and `FloorEngine._hasDirectIdleProof` (`floor_engine.dart:604-612`) both contain a fast-path — `if (rosterSize <= 1) return true;` / `if (_peers.length <= 1) return true;` — that trusts the LOCAL peer's own roster size as proof of aloneness. But roster membership itself propagates asynchronously over the identical lossy/delayed transport model the whole fix exists to guard against: ORCH confirmed at `test/simulation/soak_test.dart:295-310`'s `propagateRoster()` that roster updates are delivered via `clock.schedule(networkDelay(), ...)` — a real 2-60ms-plus-loss delay per peer, the SAME model `PRESENCE` messages ride. **A peer whose own `updateRoster` callback simply hasn't fired yet still sees `_peers.length <= 1` and grants itself immediately, bypassing the entire guard** — this is the same conceptual hole the fix was built to close (self-grant with no proof of aloneness), just relocated from "no PRESENCE info yet" to "no roster info yet," because both signals now share one asynchronous channel. This matches GB's own root-cause (1) in its Test_Evidence ("join-then-PTT at 50 ms before updateRoster lands, engine still roster={self}, solo-grants") precisely — ORCH's independent trace corroborates GB's diagnosis rather than contradicting it.

**GB's TWO ADDITIONAL DIAGNOSED CAUSES ARE CREDITED BUT NOT INDEPENDENTLY RE-TRACED BY ORCH** — recorded here for whoever resumes this task, not verified at the same depth as (3) above: (2) "delayed `TX_GRANT` from a still-blind arbiter under 2-60ms delay + 0-20% loss" and (3) one `crashed peer … still held` at seed=135. Given GB's demonstrated accuracy on (1), these are plausible and should be the starting point for the fix, but ORCH did not trace them to source the way it did (1).

**WHAT IS GENUINELY SOLID AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DESIGNED.** ORCH read the mechanism in full, not just the failing edge: `PRESENCE` correctly carries optional `holder`/`leaseRemainingMs`, populated from live state (`_emitPresence`, `floor_engine.dart:626-651`) not a cache; the guard is a genuine elapsed-time gate against the real clock (`!now.isBefore(joinedAt.add(presenceHeartbeat))`), immune to a PRESENCE-burst as the criterion demanded; a join-guarded local arbiter correctly resolves to `TX_DENY(BUSY)` via `_denyBusy` rather than a silent drop or hang; and GB built an unprompted extra safety net ORCH had not asked for — `_onPresence`'s self-heal at `floor_engine.dart:681-698`, which yields a solo grant mid-TX if a later `PRESENCE` reveals a remote live lease during the guard window, closing a race the base guard alone would have missed. Criterion 7 (§8.5 latency unaffected) is genuinely satisfied: the dedicated test `'after the guard window, TX_REQ→TX_GRANT is still synchronous'` exists (`floor_engine_test.dart:482`) and ORCH's own source read of `requestTransmit`/`_arbitrate` confirms no new `await`/async gap was introduced into the grant hot path — the new checks are synchronous boolean evaluations.

**FIX DIRECTION, NOT A MANDATE — the resuming builder should design this, not copy it verbatim.** The `rosterSize <= 1` / `_peers.length <= 1` fast-paths need to stop treating "I currently see nobody else" as equivalent to "I have been alone long enough to be sure" — those are only the same fact when roster convergence is instantaneous, which this transport model explicitly is not. The existing elapsed-time gate already used for the non-alone case is the right primitive; the alone-case fast-path needs to be brought under the same discipline (e.g., only trust apparent aloneness once the same `presenceHeartbeat` window has elapsed, or once genuine convergence is confirmed) rather than removed outright, since a genuinely-first, genuinely-alone peer must still be able to grant itself with the current low latency.

**ROOT CAUSE OF THE SECOND-ROUND RESIDUAL, TRACED TO SOURCE, NOT ASSUMED FROM GB'S "under loss + partitions" DESCRIPTION.** GB's own diagnosis names the remaining 44-45 as occurring "after the 5s join window under 0-20% loss + partitions when the peer has no live holder." ORCH checked whether the harness's partitions are permanent or recoverable — they are recoverable: `sim_network.dart`'s `setPartitioned` is TOGGLED on a random peer mid-run (`soak_test.dart:422-425`, "Toggle a network partition… link outage"), so a partitioned peer reconnects later in the same run rather than staying isolated forever. **This matters for the fix: the join-guard's elapsed-time check (`_joinedAt.add(presenceHeartbeat)`) measures raw wall-clock time since the peer joined, with no accounting for time spent partitioned.** A peer partitioned for the entire guard window has satisfied "5 seconds have elapsed since I joined" purely by clock time the moment it reconnects, despite having received zero `PRESENCE`/roster information during that window — the exact blind-self-grant condition the guard exists to prevent, just triggered by partition-then-heal instead of a slow join. **This is a well-scoped, closable gap, not evidence the ratified option A design cannot reach zero** — the fix is to gate on time since the peer was last ABLE to actually receive information (equivalently: pause/reset the guard clock while partitioned, or gate on genuine convergence rather than raw elapsed time), not to reach for options B or C. ORCH deliberately checked this before rendering a verdict, specifically to rule out sending GB after a target that the ratified spec amendment cannot actually achieve — it can.

**NOT INDEPENDENTLY RE-TRACED, CREDITED FROM GB'S DIAGNOSIS:** the precise mechanism of "delayed `_onGrant` while blind" and "`_arbitrate` self-grant while blind" as the two code paths producing the residual. GB's demonstrated accuracy on both prior root-causes (constructor-roster race, crashed-lease class) makes this credible, but ORCH has not traced these two specific paths to source the way it traced the partition-clock gap above.

**WHAT REMAINS SOLID FROM THE FIRST ROUND, UNCHANGED AND NOT RE-LITIGATED:** PRESENCE holder/lease fields, the base elapsed-time guard mechanism, `TX_DENY(BUSY)` resolution, the unprompted `_onPresence` self-heal, and criterion 7's latency guarantee (dedicated test still present, still passing) — none of these needed rework and none should be touched again.

**FIX DIRECTION FOR THE THIRD ROUND, NOT A MANDATE.** The guard's notion of "time since joined" needs to become "time since last had a live connection to the network" — during any interval `network.isPartitioned(localPeer)` would report true, the guard clock should not advance. A peer that spends its entire guard window partitioned should still be guarded on reconnection; a peer that was never partitioned should be unaffected. This does not require a new message type or protocol amendment — it is an implementation-side clock-pausing concern, entirely within `lib/core/floor/**`'s existing territory.

**TEST_EVIDENCE HONEST BOTH TIMES.** `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` claims (520/520 first round, 526/526 second round) both independently reproduced exactly. **MAJOR RECLASSIFICATION, TRACED TO SOURCE ACROSS ALL 45 FAILING SEEDS, NOT ASSUMED.** ORCH does not believe round 3's "partition-clock-crediting" diagnosis was ever the dominant driver of the round-2→3 residual. Independent instrumented tracing (two passes: 7 seeds by hand, then all 45 failing seeds mechanically tabulated across the full 500-seed run, throwaway scratch worktrees only, nothing committed) found **TWO DISTINCT FAILURE CLASSES** hiding inside the single "double-grant" `SafetyMonitor` invariant:

1. **38/45 seeds (43/50 violations) — genuinely OUT OF TASK-031's scope.** At the instant of the bad grant, `_inJoinGuardWindow=false` in every one: the join-guard had already correctly released. Root cause is the **FR-025 emergency-preemption path has no ack/handshake** — `Arbiter.decide` (`arbiter.dart:53-61`) grants an emergency requester over a known live holder unconditionally, and when the preempting peer is itself the arbiter, `_arbitrate`'s `ArbiterGrant` case (`floor_engine.dart` `_enterTx()`) fires synchronously at 0ms while the old holder's `TxGrant`/preemption notice is still in flight over `SimNetwork`'s real 2-60ms per-hop delay — a genuine simultaneous-belief window, structurally unrelated to join timing. This is **exactly the carve-out criterion 6 was written for** ("if any other, unrelated failure class remains, name it explicitly rather than silently absorbing it into this criterion") — named here, NOT charged against TASK-031, and NOT to be fixed by further join-guard iteration (no amount of `_observingSince`/`_linkPaused` tuning touches the emergency-preemption path at all). **This needs its own successor task after TASK-031 merges** (same `lib/core/floor/**` territory, so it cannot run concurrently) — raising the design question (ack-gated preemption vs. bounded local delay vs. accept-and-detect) to the project owner before scoping, the same way finding (ll)'s three options were costed and ratified, rather than ORCH unilaterally picking an approach.
2. **7/45 seeds (7/50 violations: seeds 124, 177, 260, 270, 348, 359, and one of seed 423's two occurrences) — squarely IN TASK-031's scope, NOT closed.** `_inJoinGuardWindow=true` at the instant of the bad grant in every one of these — the guard was supposed to still be blocking, but the peer accepted an incoming remote `TX_GRANT` naming itself anyway, all with `knownLiveHolder=true` and emergency-priority=true. Root cause not yet fully traced to a single line, but the shape strongly implicates the round-3 self-grant-ignore check added in `_onGrant` (`floor_engine.dart`, `if (_inJoinGuardWindow && live == null && !_hasDirectIdleProof) { ignore }`) — it only fires when `live == null`; these 7 cases all have `live != null` (a known holder), so a guarded peer with a KNOWN live holder that receives a same-named `TX_GRANT` sails straight through uninspected. Plausible mechanism: a stale/in-flight grant for an earlier request, arriving just as round-3's `_linkPaused`/partition-heal logic re-opens a fresh guard window, hits this gap. **This is the actual remaining work for TASK-031 round 4** — do not spend it on the emergency-preemption class above.

**REVISED FIX DIRECTION FOR ROUND 4, NARROW AND FINAL BEFORE RE-ESCALATING.** Close the `_onGrant` self-target gap: a guarded peer (`_inJoinGuardWindow=true`) must not enter TX on an incoming self-targeted `TX_GRANT` merely because `live == null` was the only condition checked — the check must also cover `live != null` (known-holder) cases, consistent with the guard's whole purpose (a guarded peer does not yet trust its own view of the floor, full stop, regardless of whether that view currently shows a holder or not). Re-run the exact same verification this round used (full soak, all seeds, `_inJoinGuardWindow` state tabulated at every flagged violation) — the target is **zero** seeds with `_inJoinGuardWindow=true` at a flagged grant; the 38-seed emergency-preemption class is EXPECTED to remain and must NOT be chased.

**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.3 (LOCAL path), §11 E4 (KRX-032); lib/services/mesh/mesh_controller.dart (the `final MeshFloorTransport floorTransport = MeshFloorTransport();` field — the defect this task exists to fix); 2026-08-21 ORCH integration recon (recorded in TASK-035's Description)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/services/mesh/**, test/services/mesh/**, dossiers/TASK-032.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-021
**Description:** `MeshController` requires a `FloorEngine` in its constructor, but a `FloorEngine` requires a `FloorTransport` in ITS constructor — and `MeshController.floorTransport` is a non-injectable `final` field initializer, so the engine handed to `MeshController` can structurally never be the engine attached to its own mesh transport. This makes the entire LOCAL voice path un-composable from host code and is the single hardest blocker to a two-phone test. Fix: `MeshController` gains an optional `MeshFloorTransport? floorTransport` constructor parameter (defaulting to constructing its own, so every existing test and call pattern stays valid), enabling the host sequence: construct `MeshFloorTransport` → construct `FloorEngine(transport: it)` → construct `MeshController(floorEngine: engine, floorTransport: it, ...)`. Keep the change surgical — no redesign of `MeshController`'s ownership or lifecycle semantics; it still disposes what it owns (decide and document whether an injected transport is caller-owned or controller-owned; caller-owned is the recommendation, matching Flutter convention for injected dependencies).
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] `MeshController` accepts an injected `MeshFloorTransport`; omitting it preserves today's behaviour byte-for-byte (all existing mesh tests pass unmodified)
- [x] A new test proves the composed cycle: one `MeshFloorTransport` → `FloorEngine` over it → `MeshController` given both → a floor message sent via the engine reaches the data channel path and vice versa (fake adapter fine — this is the wiring proof, not a device test)
- [x] Ownership rule (who disposes an injected transport) is documented in dartdoc AND asserted by a test (double-dispose must not throw or leak)
- [x] Full `flutter test` green, `flutter analyze` clean

**Title:** SFX projection layer — RadioState/FloorEffect → SfxEngine, the §8.2-compliant sound driver
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** high

**Owned_Paths:** lib/services/sound/**, test/services/sound/**, dossiers/TASK-034.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-010, TASK-028
**Description:** Nothing anywhere maps radio events to sounds — TASK-024's link chirps and the FaceScreen SFX gap are both this one missing layer, ratified twice as ORCH sequencing debt. Build `lib/services/sound/` (NEW territory): a projection class that (a) observes `RadioState` transitions (constructor takes a state stream or a `Listenable`; do NOT import Riverpod here — plain streams keep it host-agnostic and testable) and `FloorEngine.effects` (a broadcast stream; `RadioStateBridge` and `MeshController` already co-subscribe), and (b) calls the already-built `SfxEngine` API: `play(SfxId)`, `playRoger(variant)`, `applySquelch(detent)`, `setBedLevel`, `tick`. Event map (each row cites its spec line — verify against spec text, not this summary): power on/off → `powerOn`/`powerOff`; tune commit → `tuneBurst`; TX denied → `denyBuzz`; TOT warn/cut → `totWarn`/`totCut`; link degraded/resolved → `linkLost`/`linkUp`; emergency pinned → `emgAlert`; RX start/end → `squelchOpen`/`squelchTail` + roger on remote end per FR-062; squelch detent changes → `applySquelch`. Settings integration: subscribe to the settings `changes` stream and map `RogerBeepVariant`→`RogerVariant`, `squelchLevel`→detent — the two enum families are deliberately separate territories; your mapping is the single point of truth, document it. **`GrantTone` has NO manifest entry (known §7.1 spec gap, TASK-010 finding 6)** — map it to an existing SfxId as a disclosed pinned decision (recommend `keyClick`: mechanical, cosmetic-bus, no duck) and record it in the README's decisions table; do NOT add assets or SfxIds (frozen manifest, ORCH owes the spec amendment). This task does NOT touch `FaceScreen` — TASK-037 instantiates your class; you ship it with a pure-Dart test double (`RecordingAudioSink` is already ideal).
