**Review_Findings:** REWORK (2026-08-21T20:20Z, ORCH). **A real, reproducible double-play defect, not a nitpick — verified empirically, not just read from source.** `SfxProjection` subscribes to BOTH `RadioState` transitions AND `FloorEngine.effects` directly (per this task's own Description), but three floor effects are ALSO translated by `RadioStateBridge` into RadioEvents that flip the exact state flags `_onState` independently watches — so a single logical event plays its sound TWICE in the real running app (once from `_onFloorEffect`, once from the resulting state transition `_onState` sees on the SAME state stream TASK-037 will feed it): **`DenyBuzz` → `denyBuzz`** (FloorEffect handled directly at `sfx_projection.dart`'s `_onFloorEffect` `DenyBuzz()` case AND via `RadioStateBridge`'s `DenyBuzz → TransmitDeniedIndicated()` → `isTransmitDenied` flip → `_onState`'s `!previous.isTransmitDenied && state.isTransmitDenied` check), **`TotWarn` → `totWarn`** (same shape via `TotWarningRaised()` → `isTotWarning` flip), and **`EmgPinned` → `emgAlert`** (same shape via `EmergencyPinned()` → `isEmergency` flip). `TotCut`/`ArbiterChanged`/`EmgCleared` are NOT affected — no corresponding `_onState` edge watches their resulting flags, confirmed by reading `_onState` in full, not assumed by symmetry.

**INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED, NOT TAKEN FROM SOURCE-READING ALONE.** CX's own test suite never combines the two input streams — `sfx_projection_test.dart`'s two relevant tests each construct their OWN `_Harness` and feed EITHER `states` OR `effects`, never both on the same projection instance, which is exactly why this didn't surface: the isolation that makes the tests clean also hides the exact interaction TASK-037 will create. ORCH built a throwaway scratch worktree (`../wt-scratch-verify-034`, detached at `08e1677`, removed after — main repo and all three active builder worktrees confirmed untouched via `git status --short`/`git worktree list` before and after), added ONE combined test feeding a `DenyBuzz` effect and its resulting `isTransmitDenied` state flip into the same `SfxProjection`, and it failed exactly as predicted: **`denyBuzz` played 2 times for 1 logical event** (`Expected: <1> / Actual: <2>`). Nothing was committed; the scratch test was throwaway.

**WHAT IS GENUINELY SOLID, VERIFIED, AND SHOULD NOT BE RE-DESIGNED.** The mapping table itself is correct and complete against the task's own spec-cited event list; the `GrantTone`→`keyClick` interim decision is properly pinned in the README with the §7.1 gap cited (matches TASK-010 finding 6 exactly); settings live-update (roger/squelch/DSP) works and is tested with a real settings-stream update mid-session; the pure-Dart import guard is present and correct (no Riverpod/Flutter imports); duck-vs-no-duck sequencing for the tested paths is correct (`keyClick` doesn't duck, programme SFX ducks once). Territory clean (5 files, all in `Owned_Paths`), single commit `[TASK-034]`-tagged, PLAN.md discipline clean.

**FIX DIRECTION, NOT A MANDATE.** Pick ONE canonical source per event that both effects-and-their-state-translation cover, not both: either (a) drop the three overlapping cases from `_onFloorEffect` (`DenyBuzz`, `TotWarn`, `EmgPinned`) and let `_onState`'s existing edge-detection be the sole trigger for all three (simplest — `_onState` already has the right edges for all three), or (b) drop the corresponding `_onState` edges and rely solely on the floor effects (loses the sound if some other RadioEvent source ever sets those same flags outside the floor-effect path — check whether that's possible before choosing this route). Whichever is chosen, add a combined-stream test (state stream AND effects stream on ONE projection instance, mirroring TASK-037's real wiring) so this exact interaction class has a permanent regression guard — the two-harness-isolation test structure that hid this must not be the only coverage going forward.
**Review_Findings:** **APPROVED AND MERGED (2026-08-21T21:40Z, ORCH) as merge commit into master — the rework closes the exact gap named.** Fix (`08c245e`) took option (a): `DenyBuzz`/`TotWarn`/`EmgPinned` now break (no-op) in `_onFloorEffect`, leaving `_onState`'s existing edge-detection as the sole trigger for all three; `TotCut`/`GrantTone` correctly stay `_onFloorEffect`-only (no `_onState` edge exists for either, verified, not assumed). CX added the required combined-stream regression test (`'plays bridged floor effects once when state and effect streams combine'`) — feeds a floor effect AND its resulting state transition into ONE projection instance, mirroring TASK-037's real wiring, exactly the coverage class the two-harness isolation was missing.

**INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED THREE WAYS, NOT TAKEN FROM CX'S CLAIM.** (1) Ran the branch's own `test/services/sound` in a scratch worktree: 6/6 passed. (2) **Mutation-proved the regression guard actually guards** — reverted the fix in a throwaway copy (reinstated the old buggy `_onFloorEffect` cases) and re-ran: the new combined test failed exactly as it should, showing each of `denyBuzz`/`totWarn`/`emgAlert` played twice (`[denyBuzz, denyBuzz, totWarn, totWarn, emgAlert, emgAlert]`) — the same shape as the original defect, confirming this is a real regression guard, not a test that merely passes. (3) Reverted the mutation, ran the full branch suite: 1001 passed, 0 failed, 40 skipped (unchanged parked FR-025 count), analyze clean. Merged-tree re-verification on master: analyze clean, full suite **1001 passed, 0 failed, 40 skipped** — no regression from the merge. Territory clean throughout (5 files, all `Owned_Paths`), single fix commit `[TASK-034]`-tagged.

### TASK-035
**Title:** Radio session layer — host composition: mode selection, LOCAL chain sequencing, LINKED lifecycle, roster feed
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.3 (LOCAL sequence), §8.4 (LINKED), FR-040 + D9/KRX-062 (AUTO = LINKED-preferred for Phase 1 — D9 overrides FR-040's older prefer-LAN wording, cite D9), FR-041/FR-042 (discovery), FR-045 (fallback), FR-046 (force-LOCAL enforcement — note: FR-046 is the privacy toggle, NOT replay; replay is FR-065), §11 E7 (KRX-060/062); lib/services/{discovery,signaling,mesh,linked}/** public APIs and lib/core/settings/** (read-only consumers — NOT in your territory)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/services/session/**, test/services/session/**, dossiers/TASK-035.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-032, TASK-036
**Description:** The missing host-composition layer four territories independently disclaim. Build `lib/services/session/` (NEW territory): a `RadioSessionController` that owns the full transport lifecycle and exposes exactly what `FaceScreen` needs. Responsibilities: (1) **LOCAL chain, in the only order that works**: `IoSignalingEndpoint` → `SignalingService.start(SignalingConfig)` → read `boundPort` → `NsdDiscoveryService.production().start(DiscoveryConfig(signalingPort: boundPort, channelHashPrefix: ChannelHashPrefix.compute(...)))` → `onTuned()` on every retune → `attachDiscovery`. (2) **Floor composition via TASK-032's seam**: `MeshFloorTransport` → `FloorEngine(transport: it, tot/busyLockout from settings)` → `MeshController(floorEngine:, floorTransport:, ...)`. (3) **LINKED**: construct `TokenClient(baseUrl: from settings)` + `LiveKitClientAdapter` + `LinkedController(relayUrl: from settings, dispatch: injected)`; joins via `joinNumbered` (region/channel/code from state+settings) and `joinRoomId` (Event QR payloads — expose a `joinEvent(EventLinkPayload)` method). (4) **Mode policy per D9**: `local` → LOCAL only; `linked` → LINKED only; `auto` → LINKED when a relay URL is configured and reachable, LOCAL otherwise/on fallback; `forceLocalOnly=true` overrides ALL of it to LOCAL (never construct `LinkedController` at all when set — its own `ForceLocalOnlyException` is the backstop, not the mechanism). Dispatch `SetMode` reflecting the ACTIVE mode — and note the reducer trap: `SetMode` is only honoured in `RadioPhase.idle`, so queue it across non-idle phases. (5) **Roster feed**: `signaling.sessionsJoined/Departed` → `engine.updateRoster(peerIds)` + `bridge.updateRoster(count)` + expose `Stream<List<StationInfo>>` (peerId, callsign from `PeerSession`, signalQuality from a fixed S5 placeholder until KRX-035 telemetry lands — pin that as a disclosed decision). Retune = full teardown/rebuild of the channel-scoped parts (hash prefix changes). Expose: the `FloorEngine`, the stations stream, `joinEvent`, `retune(channel, code)`, `dispose`. All dependencies constructor-injected and fake-able; NO Riverpod imports (host-agnostic, same rule as TASK-034); `FaceScreen` wiring is TASK-037's, not yours.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] LOCAL chain sequencing proven by test with fakes: signaling started before discovery config is built, `boundPort` flows through, `onTuned` fires on retune, teardown order is the reverse
- [ ] The TASK-032 composed floor cycle is used (the engine attached to the mesh transport IS the engine handed to `MeshController`) — asserted, not assumed
- [ ] Mode matrix tested: {local, auto+relay-configured, auto+no-relay, linked} × forceLocalOnly {on, off} — 8 cases, each asserting which controllers get constructed and which never do; forceLocalOnly=on constructs NO linked-path object
- [ ] `SetMode` idle-phase queueing tested (dispatch during `tx` does not lose the mode change)
- [ ] Roster: two fake sessions joining/departing drive `updateRoster` sets, bridge counts, and the `StationInfo` stream, all asserted
- [ ] Full `flutter test` green, `flutter analyze` clean

What holds up under mutation, proved not assumed. I ran four mutations in an isolated scratch worktree (`wt-mutcheck-037`, detached at `2695751`; both real worktrees untouched, restored clean after):

- **(d) the `_sessionGeneration` guard BITES.** Weakening `if (!mounted || myGeneration != _sessionGeneration)` back to `if (!mounted)` fails exactly one test — the re-entrancy test — with `hasLength(2)` undisposed sessions against an expected 1. That is a real regression guard on a real leak, and the fix itself is the right shape.
- **(b) the pause/resume half BITES.** Deleting `pauseAutoFlip()` from both `_onScanQr`/`_onExportQr` and neutering `.whenComplete(resumeAutoFlipFresh)` fails exactly one test — the panel has auto-flipped away by the time the scan route pops, `keryx-station-panel` finds zero widgets. Correct fix, correct guard.
- **(c)** the `audioSinkDisposeCalled` recorder is a genuine assertion over a genuine seam; the `FakeAsync`-fails-on-pending-`Timer.periodic` reasoning you wrote into the comment is correct and worth having stated.
- **(e)**, taken on unprompted, is right on all three sites, and routing `joinEvent` through `_joinEvent(scanContext, ...)` so the `SnackBar` lands on the scan route's own `ScaffoldMessenger` is the correct context choice, not the lazy one.

BLOCKING (both are test-efficacy defects — the code under them is fine):

(h) **The DS L134 touch-target test cannot fail, and its comment claims it enforces the rule.** Mutation: reverting BOTH `IconButton` `BoxConstraints` from 48 back to `minWidth: 28, minHeight: 28` leaves all 14 tests green. Mechanism: the `Key` is on the `IconButton` itself, and under Material 3 `IconButton` routes through `ButtonStyleButton`'s `_InputPadding` with `MaterialTapTargetSize.padded`, which pads the widget's own render box to a 48x48 minimum *regardless of the `constraints` you pass*. So `tester.getSize(find.byKey('keryx-station-panel-scan'))` returns >= 48 unconditionally — the assertion at L483-L493 is structurally incapable of failing on the value it purports to check. This is worse than the round-1 state I described ("the repo has NO tap-target guideline test anywhere"), because the repo now has one that looks like it closed the gap and did not. The comment above it ("DS L134 is checked exactly where this task's own fix (a) applies") is, as written, false. Fix: assert something that actually varies with the constraints — the simplest honest options are (i) `tester.widget<IconButton>(find.byKey(...)).constraints` asserted against `>= 48` on both axes, or (ii) `meetsGuideline(tester, androidTapTargetGuideline)` scoped to the panel subtree if you can scope it. Whichever you choose, re-run the 48->28 mutation yourself and confirm it goes red before resubmitting; if it does not go red, the test still is not a test.

(i) **The `onInteraction` reachability test cannot fail either, and its comment states a falsehood about the timeline.** Mutation: deleting `onInteraction: flipController.flipToStations` from `face_view.dart` L175 leaves all 14 tests green. Mechanism: `autoFlipBackAfter` is 5 s; `_flipToStations` taps STN and `pumpAndSettle`s (~0 elapsed), the test does a pointer down/up, then pumps 4 s — total ~4 s, still inside the *original* window. The timer would not have fired even with no interaction reset at all, so nothing about `onInteraction` is exercised. The comment "without the fix, the panel would already have auto-flipped back to the glass display by the time this taps" is not true of the timeline the test actually runs. Fix: advance the clock past the original window *before* interacting — pump ~4 s, then the pointer-down, then pump ~4 s more (~8 s total, well past 5 s from the STN tap), and assert the icon is still findable and still tappable. Same instruction as (h): run the deletion mutation yourself and confirm red.

NON-BLOCKING:

(j) **`GlassFlipController.resumeAutoFlipFresh` does not check `_disposed`.** `pauseAutoFlip`/`flipToGlass`/`notifyListeners` are all disposal-safe, but `resumeAutoFlipFresh` gates only on `_showStations` — so if the controller is disposed while a QR route is still open, the `.whenComplete` callback arms a fresh `Timer` on a dead controller. The consequence is benign today (the timer fires once into a `_disposed`-guarded `notifyListeners`), but it is a pending timer past `dispose()`, which is exactly the class `testWidgets`' `FakeAsync` exists to catch, and the next person to widen the test coverage will trip on it. One-line `if (_disposed) return;` at the top.

(k) **Your (a) dossier note reports that a whole-screen `meetsGuideline(androidTapTargetGuideline)` run found "several other pre-existing, out-of-territory tap targets below 48 dp elsewhere on the face (the PTT key row's compact keys, the STN status-strip button)."** That is a genuinely useful find and I am taking it as ORCH debt, not charging it to you — but in light of (h)'s `_InputPadding` mechanism, please state in the dossier which widgets those actually were and what the failure message said, so the successor task starts from evidence rather than from a recollection. If it turns out M3 padding already satisfies them too, say that instead; a retracted finding recorded honestly is worth more than a vague one left standing.

(f)/(g) stand as recorded in round 1 and remain out of scope. On (f): ORCH will add "settings key reaches the back panel" to TASK-039's manual-check list directly rather than routing it through your dossier — no action for you.

**Process note, positive and worth recording:** your round-1 response was disciplined — same branch, no re-claim, every finding answered in order, the two disclosed decisions pinned in the dossier with reasoning rather than asserted, and (e) picked up unprompted because it was cheap. The two defects here are the same defect twice: a test written to describe the fix rather than to fail without it. Both of your *discriminating* tests ((b)-pause/resume and (d)) were written the other way round, so this is a consistency gap, not a capability one. Before resubmitting anything on this project again, run the one-line mutation against your own new test — revert the fix, watch it go red, put the fix back. That habit alone would have caught both of these.

### TASK-038
**Title:** Runtime permissions + foreground service lifecycle wiring
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md L368 (foreground service `mediaPlayback`+`microphone`, partial wake lock during RX/TX only, MulticastLock only while LOCAL discovery active), §11 E9 (KRX-080), KRX-092 (permission explainers — minimal viable form here, full first-run flow is a later wave); lib/services/platform/** (TASK-026's `RadioServiceController` — built, tested, invoked by nobody); lib/services/discovery/README.md (runtime `NEARBY_WIFI_DEVICES` grant is "the caller's job when the radio powers on")
**Owned_Paths:** lib/features/face/**, test/features/face/**, dossiers/TASK-038.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-037
**Description:** Same territory as TASK-037, strictly AFTER it (serial reopen — never concurrent). (1) **Permissions**: on power-on, request `RECORD_AUDIO` (blocking — the radio is dead without it; denied → a non-modal on-face treatment consistent with FR-045's never-a-modal rule, e.g. a telltale/dot-matrix line, pin the design as a disclosed decision), `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (API 33+, non-blocking), `NEARBY_WIFI_DEVICES` (API 33+, needed for NSD, non-blocking but discovery won't start without it — surface the same way). Use `permission_handler` (TASK-033 allocated it). (2) **Foreground service**: `RadioServiceController.production()` — `start(channelLabel: 'CH XX · YY')` on power-on, `setPhase` mapping `rxActive→rx`, `tx/txRequest→tx`, else `idle` on every phase transition, `updateNotification` on retune, `stop()` on power-off AND on `RadioServicePowerOffAction`; `RadioServicePttAction` → same path as the on-screen PTT press; `RadioServiceKilled` → dispatch the honest state (radio off), no silent zombie. (3) Lifecycle: service events subscription disposed with the screen; `start()` failure (`RadioServiceFailed`) degrades gracefully — radio still works foregrounded, log + telltale, never a crash.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [x] Permission request sequence on power-on tested with a fake permission layer: granted path, RECORD_AUDIO-denied path (radio does not enter idle; on-face treatment shown; NO modal), API<33 path (no POST_NOTIFICATIONS/NEARBY requests attempted) *(the API<33 sub-scenario is tested at the `ensurePermissionOutcome` unit level — `permission_gate_test.dart` — rather than through `FaceScreen`; see that test's dartdoc + the dossier for why: the behaviour is entirely inside `DeviceFacePermissionGate`'s status-check-before-request algorithm, not in anything `FaceScreen` itself branches on)*
- [x] Service lifecycle tested with `FakeRadioServicePlatform`: start-on-power-on, per-phase `setPhase` mapping (all of rx/tx/idle asserted), retune → `updateNotification`, power-off → `stop` *(disclosed deviation: power-off does NOT call `stop()` — see dossier "Event handling"; `ChannelRadioServiceController`'s own facade tests prove native has already stopped itself by the time this event reaches Dart, so `.stop()` would be a same-tick no-op. Test instead asserts `PowerOff` dispatched + `service.running == false`, matching the killed-event precedent)*
- [x] All three `RadioServiceEvent`s handled and tested: PTT action drives a real transmit request, power-off action powers off, killed → state reflects reality *(four handled, not three — `RadioServiceFailed` is the fourth sealed-class case, covered by its own criterion below)*
- [x] `RadioServiceFailed` path: no crash, radio functional, condition surfaced on-face
- [x] Full `flutter test` green, `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter build apk --debug` succeeds

That APK is the two-phone demo artifact. Non-blocking items (b), (d), (e) from round 1 stand as recorded and are carried to the KRX-092 first-run successor; (f) noted. Round-1 findings retained below. **REWORK (round 1, 2026-08-23T05:55:00Z, reviewed on claude-fable-5). One blocking finding; no other code change required.** VERIFIED, not taken from the builder's note: territory clean (9 files, pathspec-exclusion diff empty); PLAN.md discipline clean (`df785b5`/`7bb7e55` — every changed line a builder-mutable field of this block, zero frontmatter, zero other blocks); c8b9872 preflight pasted verbatim; Test_Evidence reproduced digit-for-digit by an independent run in your worktree — `flutter test` 1064 passed / 0 failed / 40 skipped, `flutter analyze` 8 issues all in TASK-035's test file, `flutter build apk --debug` succeeded (252,353,128 bytes). Face tests cross-checked statically (63 on the branch vs 47 on master = +16 = the suite delta) because the reporter's per-file listing was truncated — the known tooling artifact, not charged. Mutation-tested in an isolated scratch worktree at `f334053` (baseline 24/24 in `face_screen_test.dart`): (1) drop the `if (!_micPermissionDenied)` gate → only the RECORD_AUDIO-denied test red; (2) drop `PowerOff` in the `RadioServiceKilled` branch → only the power-off/killed test red; (3) drop the `updateNotification` block in `_retuneSession` → only the retune test red; (4) map `rxActive` to `idle` → only the phase-mapping test red; (5) drop the `RadioServiceFailed` telltale → only the post-boot-fault test red. Five real regression guards. Also verified at source: `ChannelRadioServiceController.dispose()` awaits `stop()` → `_platform.stop()`, so the only app-side power-off path that exists (widget teardown) does stop the FGS, and your disclosed deviation on `RadioServicePowerOffAction` (native already stopped; `isRunning` already false) is correct, not a shortcut. Inherited (j) is FIXED (`_disposed` guard in `resumeAutoFlipFresh`) and (k) is restated honestly in the dossier — both closed. BLOCKING: (a) **First launch on Android 13+ never boots.** `face_screen.dart` `_boot` runs `await Future.wait([permissionGate.ensureNotifications(), permissionGate.ensureNearbyWifiDevices()])`. On a first launch on API 33+ both statuses are not-granted, so both escalate to `permission_handler`'s `.request()` in the same microtask. The plugin's Android side serialises nothing and rejects an overlapping request outright — `permission_handler_android-14.0.0/.../PermissionManager.java:335`: `if (pendingRequestCount > 0) { errorCallback.onError(..., "A request for permissions is already running, please wait for it to finish before doing another request (note that you can request multiple permissions at the same time).") }` — which surfaces as a `PlatformException` from the second future. `Future.wait` then rejects; `_boot` is launched as `unawaited(Future.microtask(_boot))` (L266) with no catch around the permission phase, so the exception escapes and `_boot` aborts BEFORE the sink, session, `PowerOn` and `radioService.start()` ever run. The face renders but the radio is dead. The second launch works (the status check short-circuits), which is exactly why it will never reproduce on a dev phone that has granted once — and why your fake gate, which has no concurrency constraint, cannot see it. This is the TASK-024 failure class again, one layer down: the seam is tested, the thing behind the seam has a rule the seam hides. FIX (two parts, both required): (i) never overlap plugin requests — simplest is sequential `await`s (notifications, then nearby); cleaner is a batched `ensureNonBlocking()` on the gate that calls the plugin's list form `[Permission.notification, Permission.nearbyWifiDevices].request()`, which is precisely what its own error message points at, and keep the per-permission status checks so already-granted users are still never re-prompted; (ii) a boundary: a thrown `PlatformException` (or anything) from the gate must degrade to `denied` for that permission + `debugPrint`, never escape `_boot` — the same shape you already built for `radioService.start()`. TESTS (both required, both mutation-checked by you before resubmitting — revert the fix, watch red, restore): (1) the fake gate holds `ensureNotifications` open on a `Completer`; assert `nearbyWifiCalls == 0` while it is pending, complete it, pump, assert `1` — this goes red if `Future.wait` comes back; (2) a gate whose non-blocking methods throw still boots to `idle` with `microphoneCalls == 1` and no uncaught exception (`tester.takeException()` null). NON-BLOCKING, recorded not charged: (b) The API<33 sub-criterion is proven at the `ensurePermissionOutcome` unit level by design (status-check-before-request) — accepted as disclosed. One edge for the record: `Permission.notification` on API<33 reports denied when the user has disabled notifications in system settings, so `.request()` IS reached there; the plugin handles it without a runtime dialog, so the criterion's intent (no prompt on <33) still holds. (c) Commit `f334053`'s subject lacks the `[TASK-038]` suffix (it is in the body) — second occurrence on S5 after TASK-037's `1d0b7cd`; AGENTS.md says every message ends with it. Recorded as a pattern now; a third is a protocol finding. (d) Mic-denied recovery: the phase is pinned at `boot` with `MIC REQUIRED` until process restart — a user who grants in Settings and returns sees no change. Fine for Phase 1 (disclosed in the dossier for the service-fault case; same applies here); carry to the KRX-092 first-run task. (e) When the mic is denied no `setPhase` is ever pushed (the phase never leaves `boot`), so the persistent notification shows native's default state — cosmetic, same successor. (f) The worktree carries an uncommitted `PLAN.md` that is byte-identical to master — your documented edit-locally-then-copy workflow, not stray work; noted so the next dispatch's "dirty worktree" message is understood.

**Review_Findings:** REWORK (2026-08-22T09:05Z, ORCH). **One blocking defect against otherwise excellent, genuinely-verified work.** Fix on the SAME branch (`task/TASK-040-gb`, do NOT re-claim), scoped to the single item below.

**BLOCKING: `validate.ps1` does not go green — it exits 1, and Test_Evidence claims it passed.** ORCH ran it independently: it crashes on its FIRST step, deterministically, on both Windows and bash (`validate.sh` fails identically):
```
ValueError: 'C:\Users\...\Temp\keryx-relay-validate\livekit.yaml' is not in the
subpath of 'C:\...\relay'
```
**Root cause is your own clobber fix (which was itself correct and wanted).** You redirected the render output to `$env:TEMP`, but `relay/scripts/render_config.py:97` still calls `dest.relative_to(here)`, which throws whenever the destination is outside `relay/`. **`render_config.py` has ZERO commits on this branch — the caller changed and the callee never did.** The script aborts before `docker compose config` and before the 18 unit checks ever execute, so the "render + compose config + 18 unit checks green" line in Test_Evidence cannot have come from the committed state. Not treated as dishonesty (most likely run before the temp-dir change landed), but the claim does not reproduce and `validate.ps1` is this project's standing "is the relay config sane" gate — shipping it broken is rework-level.

**SECOND, NON-BLOCKING BUT REQUIRED WITH THE FIX: the test suite structurally cannot see this failure.** `relay/tests/test_relay_config.py` passes 18/18 precisely because it never invokes `validate.ps1`/`.sh` — it re-implements the checks instead of running the gate. Add a test that actually EXECUTES the validate script and asserts exit 0, so this class of caller/callee drift is caught by the suite rather than by review. (Same lesson as TASK-024's "the unit is tested, the wiring is not".)

**EVERYTHING ELSE VERIFIED AND GENUINELY STRONG — do not re-do any of it.** ORCH reproduced independently in a throwaway scratch worktree with a real Docker bring-up (all containers cleaned up afterward, `docker ps` empty, main repo byte-identical):
1. **The end-to-end join proof is real and reproduces exactly** — garbage JWT → `HTTP 401`; minted JWT → `HTTP 101 Switching Protocols`; `ListParticipants identities=['BRAVO-7#a126ca7f']`; `JOIN OK room=ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOP`. That is a genuine join with the participant confirmed in RoomService, not an endpoint-returned-200 — exactly what the criterion demanded and the thing most likely to have been faked.
2. **The `LIVEKIT_KEYS` space bug is real, empirically confirmed, not cargo-culted.** ORCH ran LiveKit v1.9.11 both ways: no-space exits `Could not parse keys, it needs to be exactly, "key: secret", including the space` (near-verbatim your claim); with-space starts clean. Asserted by `test_livekit_keys_env_has_required_space` too. **This bug alone would have silently broken every LINKED field test** — finding it is the highest-value thing in this submission.
3. **Rate limiting confirmed** — ORCH got `successes=29 limited=3 last=429` vs your 28/4; both sum to 32 against `RATE_LIMIT_MAX=30`, ordering variance only, and a spurious 429 on a rerun inside the window was incidental extra proof.
4. **Caddy `/token` works end-to-end** via `Caddyfile.local` (`Caddy /token path minted identity=BRAVO-7#a95cde75`) — the 503 placeholder is genuinely gone and the route matches TASK-036's `https://HOST/token` convention.
5. **The clobber fix is real and correctly targeted** — a pre-existing `generated/` can no longer be overwritten by validate. (Direct `render_config.py` invocation still writes `relay/generated` by design; that is intended, not the bug.)
6. **`ops/TWO_PHONE_TEST.md` is high quality and honest.** 12-row triage table mapping phone-side symptom → cause → **which logs to pull**, including the subtle "Token 200 but `/rtc` 401 = key mismatch, diff the two .env files". Append-safe for TASK-039 via an explicit `<!-- TASK-039 APP-SIDE START -->` marker. Carries TS §8.7 privacy constraints unprompted (never log JWTs/callsigns/room IDs, don't raise `pion_level` in production, wipe pcaps). **Its DNS/TLS honesty is exactly right** — a dedicated "prerequisites localhost cannot prove" section stating ACME won't issue for localhost, `EXTERNAL_IP=127.0.0.1` makes coturn a blackhole, and NFR-05 is a VPS-only measurement. It even pre-rejects the weak-proof framing in its own words: *"That is a real join, not 'the mint endpoint returned 200'."*
7. Territory clean (14 files, all `Owned_Paths`), both commits `[TASK-040]`-tagged, preflight pasted verbatim, `token-svc` pytest 23/23, relay tests 18/18, **zero Dart touched** (verified: `git diff --name-only -- '*.dart'` empty).

**HONESTLY OUT OF REACH, CORRECTLY SCOPED, NOT CHARGED:** TLS/ACME on a real domain, TURN traversal / NFR-05, and the production `caddy` compose service's HTTPS path — all need a real domain and public IP. Your runbook says so itself rather than pretending localhost proved them, which is the correct disposition.

**FIX DIRECTION.** One line at `render_config.py:97` (fall back to the absolute path when `relative_to` raises, or stop computing a relative path for display when the dest is external), plus the validate-executing test. Do not touch anything else — items 1-7 above are approved as-is and should not be re-litigated.

### TASK-042
**Title:** Phase 2 PTT redesign — single hero PTT disc with a live amplitude-ring meter
**Status:** in_progress
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** Approved Phase 2 design canvas (https://claude.ai/code/artifact/885a21ca-a4f2-4605-bc9a-7a36807be74e, Main.dc.html interactive prototype — the ring-meter behaviour, disc press/release states, and glyph swap are normative here, not a written spec section) — supersedes specs/KERYX_UI_Design_Specification_v1.0.md §4 Key travel ("Pressed keys move 1 dp down... same three changes on every control" — the disc keeps this rule, only its shape and size change) and the PTT portion of §5 Signature Elements; §2 Colour Tokens (tx #E23D2E, rx #7FD1A0, lcd/idle amber #F2A93B, emergency #FF7A18 — all via KeryxTheme, no literal hexes in widget code); lib/features/ptt/** (frozen, reopened here — ptt_button.dart, key_row.dart, edge_glow.dart, emg_key.dart, ptt_haptics.dart all in territory)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/features/ptt/**, test/features/ptt/**, dossiers/TASK-042.md
**Depends_On:** —
**Description:** Second of two independent lanes that converge in TASK-043; does not touch lib/features/face/**, lib/features/display/**, lib/features/knob/**/grille/**, or lib/app.dart/lib/main.dart. Rebuilds the PTT control as the approved design's hero disc, replacing the old compact key-style PttButton. (1) New disc widget: a large circular control (approved canvas uses a 320dp outer diameter with a 236dp raised face — treat those as the reference proportions, adjust only if a real device safe-area forces it, and say so if you do) surrounded by a ring of 64 short radial ticks that light up symmetrically from the top as a live amplitude meter — the ring is driven by an external level value (0-100) the widget receives, NOT computed internally, so FaceScreen (TASK-043) can feed it real mic/RX amplitude later; ship a PttRingController/ValueListenable<double> seam (constructor-injectable, defaulting to a simple internal ticker for standalone preview/tests) rather than hardcoding a demo animation into the shipped widget. (2) State colour: idle ring+glyph amber (KeryxTheme.lcd), pressed/TX red (KeryxTheme.tx), external RX-active state green (KeryxTheme.rx), emergency state orange (KeryxTheme.emergency) — expose state as an enum the parent sets, mirroring how ptt_state.dart already models PTT state today; check that file before inventing a second state enum. (3) Press/release: reuse KeryxTheme's pressed-key convention (1 dp down, drop the top highlight, gain the inner shadow) applied to the disc's raised-face gradient, exactly as the approved canvas's Transmit artboard shows; wire through existing ptt_haptics.dart and edge_glow.dart hook points rather than duplicating haptic/glow logic. (4) Icon glyph swaps mic -> speaker outline when the parent reports RX-active (matches the canvas); centre legend text swaps PTT / BUSY / CANCEL per state, driven by the same enum. (5) key_row.dart's four-key rail (MON/SCAN/STN/EMG) stays as a separate row below the disc per the canvas — keep it, just confirm it still lays out correctly beneath the larger disc and is not a dependency of the disc itself. (6) emg_key.dart's existing emergency-hold behaviour is preserved; only the visual state colour (orange) plumbing changes to match the disc, not the hold-to-arm interaction logic itself, unless FR text you find says otherwise — if so, cite it in the dossier.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] New disc widget renders standalone in a widget test at each of idle/tx/rx/emergency, asserting ring stroke colour and centre glyph/legend per state
- [ ] Ring level is driven by an injected value/listenable, not an internal hardcoded animation — a widget test sets the level directly and asserts the number of lit ticks changes accordingly
- [ ] Press produces the 1dp-down / no-top-highlight / inner-shadow treatment (asserted via the same technique existing key_row_test.dart/ptt_button_test.dart use for other keys, or documented if a new assertion approach was needed)
- [ ] Existing ptt_haptics.dart and edge_glow.dart are reused, not duplicated — dossier states which hook points were wired
- [ ] key_row.dart (MON/SCAN/STN/EMG rail) still passes its existing tests unmodified in behaviour, laid out beneath the new disc in a combined widget test
- [ ] emg_key.dart hold-to-arm interaction is unchanged in behaviour; only its colour token changed — a before/after diff note in the dossier confirms this
- [ ] flutter analyze clean on every file this task touches; flutter test green for test/features/ptt/**

### TASK-043
**Title:** Phase 2 PTT redesign — assemble the new hero-PTT home screen, station roster screen, and emergency band
**Status:** pending
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** Approved Phase 2 design canvas (https://claude.ai/code/artifact/885a21ca-a4f2-4605-bc9a-7a36807be74e — every artboard is normative for this task: Main.dc.html layout/interaction, Transmit/Receive as state references, Emergency.dc.html's hard orange band, Roster.dc.html's station list screen); specs/KERYX_Product_Technical_Spec_v1.1.md §8.2 (single state source — the new layout must still read off one state stream, not invent a second); FR-067 (station flip panel — becomes a full roster screen, not a flip panel, per the approved canvas; superseding the flip-panel treatment in lib/features/face/glass_flip_controller.dart for station listing specifically); lib/features/face/** (frozen, reopened — face_screen.dart, face_view.dart, housing.dart, roster.dart, station_panel.dart, glass_flip_controller.dart, status_strip.dart all in territory), lib/app.dart + lib/main.dart (frozen, reopened for the new roster route)
**Owned_Paths:** lib/features/face/**, lib/app.dart, lib/main.dart, test/features/face/**, dossiers/TASK-043.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-041, TASK-042
**Description:** The convergence task — waits for both independent lanes (display strip + PTT disc) to land, then composes them into the approved layout and retires the flip-panel pattern for the main screen. (1) FaceScreen/FaceView layout changes from the current knob+glass+grille+control-cluster+PTT stack to: header bar -> TASK-041's LCD strip (fed the session's real mode/channel/telltale/status data — this is a straight rewire, the session/session_host seam from the existing face_screen.dart is untouched) -> TASK-042's hero PTT disc, centred, sized to own roughly the layout proportions the approved canvas shows -> the existing four-key rail beneath it. Delete housing.dart content that only existed to frame the knob/grille (keep whatever is still load-bearing for the shell's material/background — state in the dossier what was kept and why). (2) Wire the disc's level input to real amplitude: TX from the mic input already flowing through AmplitudeSource/the audio pipeline TASK-037 wired, RX from whatever remote-level signal already exists (if none exists yet, wire a reasonable proxy — e.g. a fixed or lightly-randomized "active" level while RX is open — and say plainly in the dossier that real RX amplitude is a later wave, matching how TASK-037's own dossier disclosed the DSP-later decision). Disc state (idle/tx/rx/emergency) comes from the same session/floor state FaceScreen already reads for its current PTT key — no new state source. (3) Station roster: roster.dart's existing StationInfo stream stays the data source, but its presentation moves from station_panel.dart's flip-panel treatment to a full-screen route (new RosterScreen or repurpose station_panel.dart's content into one, builder's call, state which in the dossier) matching Roster.dc.html — reachable from the rail's STN key (replacing whatever STN currently does) and returning to the main screen via a back action, not a re-flip. glass_flip_controller.dart's auto-flip machinery is specific to the old glass-panel pattern; if nothing else in the new layout still flips, retire it and say so, or keep it narrowly for whatever (if anything) still needs a timed reveal. (4) Emergency: when the session reports an active emergency, render the approved canvas's hard orange band under the header and drive the disc/rail into the emergency colour state from TASK-042 — reuse whatever emergency signal emg_key.dart already surfaces rather than inventing a new one. (5) lib/app.dart/lib/main.dart changes are additive only (new roster route registration) — do not touch the existing settings/QR routing TASK-037 built unless the new layout genuinely requires moving where those entry points live, in which case cite the approved canvas's header kebab-menu placement as the reason. (6) Update every test/features/face/** widget test whose assertions target now-deleted widgets (knob, grille, old flip panel) — do not leave dead assertions or silently drop coverage; a test that exercised real behaviour needs an equivalent new test, not a deletion with no replacement.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] FaceScreen no longer imports lib/features/knob/** or lib/features/grille/** (both deleted by TASK-041); no references remain anywhere in lib/features/face/**
- [ ] Main screen renders LCD strip (top) + hero PTT disc (centre) + four-key rail (bottom) using the exact widgets TASK-041/042 shipped, not re-implementations
- [ ] Disc level is fed a real value during TX (from the existing mic amplitude pipeline) and some documented value during RX; state (idle/tx/rx/emergency) is driven by the same session/floor state the old PTT key used — asserted with a faked session in a widget test for at least idle->tx and idle->rx transitions
- [ ] Station roster is reachable as a full screen from the rail's STN key and returns to the main screen; live join/depart still reflected (same guarantee TASK-037 already tested, ported to the new screen) — empty state still renders its existing copy
- [ ] Emergency band appears when the session reports an active emergency and disappears when it clears; disc/rail reflect the emergency colour state — widget-tested
- [ ] Every pre-existing test/features/face/** test either still passes unmodified or was replaced by an equivalent test for the same behaviour against the new widgets — dossier lists every deleted assertion and its replacement (or states none was needed)
- [ ] flutter analyze clean repo-wide; flutter test full suite green; flutter build apk --debug succeeds
